# Changelog

## Upgrade notes (unreleased)

* **workflows:** an operator can raise a hunt's default limits for the whole deployment (#5333). `HUNT_DEFAULT_ITERATIONS` (1–40, default 8), `HUNT_DEFAULT_COST_USD` (above 0 up to 100, default 15.0) and `HUNT_DEFAULT_WALL_SECONDS` (60–86400, default 5400) apply to every hunt whose start names no limit: chat-started, scheduled, and a run form left blank. The run form's placeholders and the preflight show them. A hunt's call budget and its hard ceilings (what Keep going may extend to) follow at twice the default, as they already followed a per-run ask. A limit set on a run still wins. A value outside its range is logged once at API start and shown on the hunt's run form, and the shipped value stands. Root-cause traces keep $15 and 90 minutes. `WORKFLOW_DEFAULT_WALL_SECONDS` does not apply to hunts. No migration. Unset, nothing changes; on Compose and Helm set them in the backend's environment.

* **response:** a tool is an emulator because its binding says so (#5315). A person binds a tool as an emulator on its row under Settings › Limits & autonomy › Outside tools (the capability select gains Emulator), and Atomic Red Team's `atomic_red_team_execute` ships bound as one in `core/integrations/bindings.yaml`. Settings › Limits & autonomy › Emulators now edits only the hosts emulators may run on. Revision `0137_emulator_bindings` gives each tool the stored `emulation.tools` marked, beyond the two Atomic spellings, the binding `{"emulation": true}` on its class row; a tool with no row gets one at `irreversible`, written by `system`, which gates it as being unclassified did. A stored list that left Atomic out unbinds it. A row already carrying a binding keeps it and the upgrade logs the mark it did not carry over. Each write goes on the trail. `tools` is then dropped from the stored value; the host list is kept. `PUT /api/v1/autonomy/tools/{name}` takes `{"binding": {"emulation": true}}` (or capability `emulation`) with the same permissions as any binding, `GET /capabilities` lists `emulation`, and each tool's binding carries `emulation`. `PUT /api/autonomy/emulation` ignores a `tools` field, and its answer no longer has `tools` or `defaults.tools`. On Compose, Helm and desktop the revision runs with the upgrade.

* **response:** a declared `INTENT.md` (`VIGIL_INTENT_DIR`) sets each investigate workflow's step-score bar beside its tier and floor: `workflows.<id>: {tier: act, floor: 85, bar: 0.6}` (#5319). `bar` is a number from 0 to 1 under either tier; leaving it out declares no bar, and `true`, a quoted number, `null` or a value outside 0..1 rejects the revision at its line. On an Act entry a higher or new bar applies on the next poll and a lower or removed one waits for a person with `ai_autonomy.write` to accept; an entry that loosens in any part waits whole. A declared Assist entry writes its bar, or none, on the next poll, since the bar holds nothing under Assist. A console or API write (`PUT /api/autonomy/workflows/{id}`) that would lower or clear a declared bar is refused with 409 naming the file, line and revision; a raise past it saves and reads as drift. The intent report shows the bar on both sides ("Act · floor 85 · bar 0.60"). With no declared directory nothing changes. No migration. Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **response:** `respond.confidence_threshold` is retired (#5279). It decided nothing since #2918: every approval class waits for a person unless the autonomy setting lets the run act. `DAEMON_CONFIDENCE_THRESHOLD` is now ignored and is gone from `env.example`, Compose and the Helm values; an install that still sets it starts as before. A declared `INTENT.md` (`VIGIL_INTENT_DIR`) that still names the key applies its other keys and logs one warning instead of rejecting the revision; the repository `INTENT.md` drops it with the same warning. An applied row that already holds the key is read past. The `$auto_approve` prompt band is gone from the built-in agents, and an operator's stored agent that still names `$auto_approve` reads `never`. `python -m services.daemon.intent --replay` (and `--since`) is gone; the CLI still prints the declared-vs-effective diff. The Workflows › Agents "Asks first" tip now says actions wait unless Settings › Limits & autonomy lets the run take them. No migration. Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop; remove `confidence_threshold` from a declared `INTENT.md` to silence the warning. See [Upgrading](docs/upgrading.md).

* **approvals:** an approval's `confidence` in `/api/v1` (`GET /api/v1/approvals` and each approval route) and in `/mcp` (`list_approval_actions`, `get_approval_action`) now carries the step score's value (`parameters.step_score.value`), or null for a row without one: rows filed before this release, and rows not filed by an investigation (checkpoints, rule deploys, undos, `create_approval_action`) (#5278). The key and its type are unchanged; it no longer carries the proposer's own number, which is no longer read. `list_approval_actions` gains `step_score` on each action, and `GET /api/v1/approvals/needs-you` items gain `step_score`. `create_approval_action` no longer takes a `confidence` (one still sent is ignored and the action files), and an investigation's `proposed_actions` no longer carry one. The case's needs block shows a containment's score (band, lowest factor and what would raise it, factors on expand) to the person deciding it. The `approval_actions.confidence` column stays as it is and is no longer written; no migration. Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **response:** each benign or false-positive dismissal an investigation reaches, and each containment it proposes, carries a step score worked out from stored records by one rule (#5275, `core/response/step_confidence.py`, rule version 1). The score is the lowest of three factors: precedent (95% Wilson lower bound of people's determinations of the case's kind agreeing with the step; nobody judged gives 0), blind spots (any call the estate failed to a required tool that no later call served gives 0) and run quality (capped, cited nothing or not grounded gives 0). It is unknown when a factor is unknown and none is 0. Revision `0132_workflow_run_step_score` adds nullable `workflow_runs.step_score` (JSONB), written once per dismissal with what the close check did; a containment's score is in its approval's `parameters.step_score`. The `agent_run_closed` and `agent_run_left_open` activities and the `case.close` trail entry quote the value, band and lowest factor. The agent's terminal gains `blind_spots` and `unserved_tools`. Nothing reads the score to decide yet: closes and releases behave as before. On Compose, Helm and desktop the revision runs with the upgrade.

* **auth:** the password minimum is 3–255 (default 16) and the strength score is a setting, 0–4 (default 3). At 0, no score is required. The minimum no longer depends on Require MFA. A policy saved before this release has no score and reads as 3, so an install that never changed a setting behaves the same. A sign-in without MFA rechecks a password shorter than the lower of 15 and the minimum; raising the minimum sends only those people to a forced change. The longest password accepted is 255. An imported breached list keeps entries down to 3 characters; import a list again for it to cover short entries. The shipped list still has none under 12. `AUTH_MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH`, where it still reaches an install with no saved policy, is carried once and clamped into 3–255; a value of exactly 12 counts as unset. `AUTH_MIN_ZXCVBN_SCORE` is not carried: the API logs that it is ignored and points at Settings › Sessions. A save that leaves the score out keeps the stored score. Lowering the score is a loosening only a person can save. No migration. Rolling back after a value outside the old ranges, or a minimum under 15 while Require MFA is not everyone, is described in `docs/upgrading.md`. Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **auth:** with Require MFA set to off, a person who holds no second factor confirms a privileged change, and creates or rotates a token, with their password (#5114). A password sign-in by that person opens the confirmation window. Under privileged roles or everyone, nothing changes: a person the setting covers is held at setup, and anyone else with no factor is sent to set one up at the change. People who already hold a factor confirm as before, under every setting. `auth.confirmed` names the factor (password, code, key or provider), `auth.sign_in` names how the person got in, and a token mint, rotate, directory sync's token and an app connection's grant name the sign-in that made them. Installs already at off change on upgrade: privileged changes and token creation now go through with the password. The count of people without MFA is the existing one (the daily `auth.mfa_coverage_gap` entry, and the number shown when lowering Require MFA). Tokens already created keep working if the setting is raised. No migration. Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **auth:** both idle limits accept 0 (no idle limit) through 720 minutes (12 hours), set independently (#5125). The defaults stay 15 and 10. A saved 0 does not end a sign-in for idleness, and raising an idle or setting 0 is a loosening. Confirm it's you follows the privileged idle limit up to 12 hours, and stays 10 minutes when that limit is 0. The Day 1 idle check judges the looser of the two and words 0 as "no idle limit". A value already inside the previous ranges behaves the same. Nothing is migrated. Rolling back to the previous release after saving an idle of 0, a value above the previous range, or a privileged limit above the standard one makes the stored policy unreadable until an administrator saves Settings > Sessions again: Sessions shows the defaults, token mint and rotate are refused, the unused-token sweep stops, and the Day 1 session checks read "Could not read the session settings." See [Upgrading](docs/upgrading.md).

* **memory:** a file import keeps each alert, one row per alert id (#5091). Revision `0120_history_alerts` adds `history_alerts` (import, alert id, time, title, description, rule id, severity, entity keys; unique on import and alert id) and `history_imports.dropped_columns` (column name to how many alert rows carried it). A later row in the same file with the same id replaces the earlier one. An alert with no id is not stored; a row with no parseable time still is. Title and description are capped prose. Disposition, classification, status, determination, owner, closer, notes, source and any other leftover column are not stored; their names and counts are on the import and in the upload's job line, and under the import in Settings › Data & uploads. Id, time and source column names are not listed as dropped. Columns that become entity keys are stored as keys. Sightings, verdicts and ticket rows are unchanged. A connected Defender or Sentinel pull still writes no alert rows. An import already stored gets no alert rows and an empty dropped-column map; nothing backfills it. Deleting the import removes the rows. On Compose, Helm and desktop the revision runs with the upgrade.

* **storage:** the platform database proxy's SSH tunnel now checks the bastion's SSH host key. Settings › System › Platform Database Proxy has a required SSH Host Key field (an OpenSSH public-key line, as in `/etc/ssh/ssh_host_ed25519_key.pub` or `ssh-keyscan` output, or a `known_hosts` line), stored as `PLATFORM_DB_SSH_HOST_KEY` in the secrets store. A server presenting any other key is refused before any login is sent. The tunnel offers only the configured password or key file: no SSH agent keys, no keys from the process user's `~/.ssh`, and no `~/.ssh/config`. Through the tunnel, Vigil no longer sends its database password in cleartext (libpq 16+; md5 and SCRAM servers still work, and `PGREQUIREAUTH` overrides this). `POST /api/config/platform-database` in SSH tunnel mode answers 400 without a readable host key, and a new host key counts as a new destination, so the database password (and the SSH password for password login) must be entered again. **An install already using the SSH tunnel stops connecting to its database after this upgrade until the host key is saved.** Get the key from the bastion itself, not over the same network path. Save it in Settings › System before upgrading; after the upgrade the console cannot reach its database, so set the `PLATFORM_DB_SSH_HOST_KEY` environment variable for every process that opens the database (backend, daemon, LLM worker), restart, and then save it in Settings › System. No migration.

* **reporting:** the Production Start Report now includes time to first verdict and time to close, per source, for the production window (#4999). Revision `0119_first_verdict_at` adds nullable `findings.first_verdict_at`. Triage sets it once, when a verdict is first recorded, and a later triage does not move it. A finding triaged before the upgrade stays null and reads "not recorded". Time to close uses the same close time as case metrics (`case_closure_info.closed_at`, else `cases.updated_at`). Median and p90 appear at 20 or more timed alerts; under 20 the report counts three bands and shows no ratio. Alerts on a restricted case, `vigil-sample` and `probe` are left out and counted. Defender, Splunk, CrowdStrike, Elastic, Security Hub, OpenSearch, Sentinel and Cloudflare keep storing the fetch time, and the report says those alerts cannot be told apart. Beside time to close, the latest Day 1 record's stored "before" time to close is copied, or the report says that record has no history sections. The section needs `findings.read`. On Compose, Helm and desktop the revision runs with the upgrade.

* **llm:** a stored model-provider key is sent only to the host it was entered for. `PUT /api/llm/providers/{id}` that changes the base URL of a provider with a stored key now needs `api_key` in the same request (400 otherwise). `PUT /api/bifrost/providers/{name}` (Settings › AI models › Edit provider) refuses, with 409, to move a provider that holds a key to another custom host: remove its keys, save the base URL, then add the key again; going back to the default host is allowed. An Ollama key write that changes the server URL needs its bearer token again, or `value: ""` for none, and a key write that sets an Azure endpoint or Bedrock block needs the key. The gateway's one OpenAI-type key and host now always come from the same provider row: the active default with a key, else the oldest active one, and a row naming a custom host counts only with its own key; a keyless row's host is used only while the gateway holds no key. A provider turned off no longer keeps or regains the gateway's key. An install that relied on a second OpenAI-type row's host carrying the default row's key now routes that type to the default row's host; give the other row its own key and make it the default, or use a separate provider type. No migration. Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **storage:** the platform database proxy's "Verify proxy TLS" setting now takes effect. In PgBouncer mode with it on (the default), Vigil connects to the pooler with `sslmode=verify-full`, trusting `PGSSLROOTCERT` when set and the system store otherwise, and refuses a pooler without TLS or with a certificate not valid for its host. With it off, TLS follows `POSTGRES_SSL_MODE` or the connection string's `sslmode` as before. The toggle is no longer shown for an SSH tunnel. Saving `POST /api/config/platform-database` with a new proxy type, host or port, or with PgBouncer TLS verification turned off, now needs `database_password` (Vigil's database password, checked and not stored) and, for a password SSH tunnel, the SSH password again; otherwise it answers 400. An install whose PgBouncer has no TLS or a self-signed certificate stops reaching the database after this upgrade: set `PGSSLROOTCERT` to the pooler's CA for every process that opens the database (backend, daemon, LLM worker), or turn the toggle off. No migration.

* **cases:** the person who closes a case now owns it (#4983). A close on the access cookie, or from a console chat turn, sets `Case.assignee` to the closer and appends a `case.assign` entry after the `case.close` entry. It skips, and the close still lands, when the closer resolves to no active account, already owns the case, cannot read a restricted case, or is not a current member of the case's team, and when an unspecified close leaves a standing determination. A close needs only `cases.write` to take a case another person owns. Agent runs, `/mcp` and API-token callers, and merges never take ownership. An owner named in the same save stands: `PATCH /api/v1/cases/{id}` and the `update_case`/`create_case` tools with an `assignee` do not take the case, and `POST /api/v1/cases/{id}/close` gains `take_ownership` (default `true`; `false` leaves the owner alone). No migration. Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **integrations:** Medic reads each federation source's severity floor, item cap and poll interval, and how many findings the floor has skipped (#4891). Revision `0112_source_health_filtered` adds `source_health.filtered_total` (integer, default 0). It counts skips, not distinct findings, survives a reset the way `dropped_total` does, and is not a drop: the health judgement does not read it. `GET /api/federation/sources` serves it. A higher floor, a lower cap or a longer interval is reported to the admin while scheduled collection is on; Medic does not change the setting. A quiet source whose floor skipped findings over the quiet window is no longer called a Vigil defect. medic-core 0.10.0 carries the rules. A Vigil that does not serve the new keys keeps today's source-silent behaviour and does not open the new reports. Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **integrations:** Splunk and CrowdStrike informational findings now rank below Low (#4893). Splunk stores urgency or severity `info` and `informational` (after lowercasing) as `info`, and CrowdStrike stores `max_severity_displayname` `Informational` as `info`. Every other value those two send is stored as before, on Alert collection and on the older poll. "Low and up" and every higher floor skip an informational finding; only Any keeps it. Revision `0108_info_below_low` clears `federation_sources.min_severity` where it is `low` and the source is Splunk or CrowdStrike, and the revision report lists those source ids under `moved_to_any`. Choosing "Low and up" again drops their informational findings. Findings already stored as `low` stay `low`. These findings now fall in the `unknown` intake band. The default `DAEMON_INVESTIGATE_SEVERITIES` includes both `low` and `unknown`, so an install that has not changed it offers them as often as before; a custom list that names only one of those two bands offers them more or less often. On Compose, Helm and desktop the revision runs with the upgrade. Read `moved_to_any` in the upgrade history, and set "Low and up" again only when informational findings should be dropped.

* **integrations:** Kafka, the daemon webhook, and the Darktrace and Cloudflare receivers write Integration Health (#4870, epic #4866). An accepted event is a success with data received. After the sender has authenticated, a bad body or a failed transform counts as an unreadable record, and a finding that was not persisted is a failure. A sender that never authenticated, and the daemon's refusal of a disabled integration, change nothing. A change of state is written at once; received and dropped counts, and the labels seen with them, flush at most once a minute per source per process. Revision `0105_source_health_labels` adds `source_health.labels` (a capped map of label to last-seen time, information only), following `0105_merge_0104_heads`. `0107_merge_0106_heads` joins it with `0106_history_imports`. Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **memory:** a history import has its own record and keeps every closed ticket it took (#4886, epic #4885). Revision `0106_history_imports` adds `history_imports` (one row per import: status `running`/`done`/`failed`, start and finish, rows read, rows left out by reason, unmapped disposition values with counts, a connected pull's days and pull time, and whether it has ticket detail) and `history_tickets` (each kept ticket, bulk-closed included: source ticket ID, its own source, opened and closed, raw disposition, category with benign apart from false positive, severity or `unknown`, rule ID, entity keys, title, a close-notes fingerprint and empty flag, the missing-open and approximate-close marks, `bulk_closed`), both granted to `vigil_app`. It copies every `investigation_kind = 'imported'` row of `episodic_distil_markers` into `history_imports` as done without ticket detail, then deletes those markers; memory's verdict and sighting rows are unchanged. Bulk-closed tickets still stay out of agent memory. No closer is stored: `closed_by`, assignee and owner are read only for the bulk-close check, and a row with no ticket ID is keyed without them (rows that differ only in their closer are both kept). Defender adds `severity` and `detectorId`; Sentinel adds `severity`, the rule ID when exactly one rule is related, and marks its close time approximate; a file's `severity` and `rule_id` columns are read. One history import runs at a time across every API process (a second start answers 409); a run whose process died lists as `incomplete` ("didn't complete") in `GET /api/memory/imports`, and the next start marks it failed. Home's history step counts only finished imports. Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **integrations:** each source's health is one stored record, Integration Health (#4869, epic #4866). Revision `0104_source_health` adds the `source_health` table (granted to `vigil_app`) and copies into it the health columns of `federation_sources` (keyed by integration id, so `elastic` lands on `elastic-siem`, and likewise Azure Sentinel, AWS Security Hub and Microsoft Defender) and `integration_configs.last_test_*`; where an integration has both, the later of the last poll and the last Test is the last attempt. Scheduled collection, Test and detection-source syncs write the record; saving an integration that is switched on, or whose settings or credential changed, resets it to "no attempt yet". `GET /api/federation/sources`, `GET /api/federation/health` and `GET /api/config/integrations` serve the same fields as before, read from the record. `GET /api/integration-health` (`integrations.read`) lists every integration, Kafka, the three webhook receivers and each repository detection source with its record and its state. `federation_sources.last_poll_at`, `last_success_at`, `last_error`, `last_error_kind`, `consecutive_errors`, `dropped_total` and `integration_configs.last_test_at`, `last_test_success`, `last_error` are no longer written and will be dropped in a later release; anything reading them by SQL should read `source_health`. Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **auth:** turning on Require single sign-on ends a person's open sign-in that didn't come through SSO, no later than its next refresh or Confirm it's you (#4841). The refresh and the confirmation answer 401 `Sign-in has ended` with `X-Sign-In-End-Reason: sso_required`, before any password or code is checked and without counting a failure or writing `auth.sign_in_ended`. The signed-out screen says "You were signed out because your organization now signs in with SSO."; Settings → Users says "Single sign-on is now required". The sign-in's runs keep going. A sign-in that came through SSO, a break-glass sign-in, a service account, and everything while the setting is off, are unchanged. Turning it off does not reopen a sign-in this ended. Revision `0101_sso_required_end_reason` adds `sso_required` to `sign_ins_end_reason_check` and leaves existing rows alone. Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **helm:** agent-serve and agent-worker no longer load the application Secret whole (#4839). They still load the chart ConfigMap. From that Secret they take `POSTGRES_AGENT_PASSWORD` (as `POSTGRES_PASSWORD`) and, only when the key is present, `AGENT_INTERNAL_TOKEN`, `AGENT_INTERNAL_TOKEN_PREVIOUS` and `BIFROST_API_KEY`. A chart-rendered install loses nothing those pods use. An operator-supplied Secret or an ExternalSecret needs no change while its key names stay the same. Any other key those pods used to read from that Secret, including `DATABASE_URL`, now belongs on `agentServe.extraEnv` and `agentWorker.extraEnv`. An `extraEnv` entry for one of those three names still overrides the Secret for that workload. Compose and the desktop app are unchanged.

* **cases:** the shared IOC index has one subject, the Case (#4815, epic #4580). Revision `0100_shared_ioc_case_only` deletes the legacy per-Investigation rows (`shared_iocs.case_id` NULL), drops `shared_iocs.investigation_id` with its index and foreign key, makes `shared_iocs.case_id` and `cases.indicators_synced_at` NOT NULL, replaces the partial `uq_shared_ioc_case_key` with a plain unique index (dropping `idx_shared_ioc_case`, which it covers) and drops `idx_case_indicators_unsynced`. Matching, the IOC list's `linked` label and the catch-up loop no longer read legacy rows or look for never-synced Cases, and the bell's "Bringing existing Cases in" line is gone. The upgrade is refused, before any revision runs and whatever the restore point or acknowledgement, while any Case has `indicators_synced_at` NULL, or when the database predates 1.1.3 and holds any Case; the reason gives the count. To get through: install 1.1.3 (or any later release without this change), let its catch-up finish (the "Bringing existing Cases in" line goes away), then upgrade again. On Helm the weight −10 `--check` refuses and the previous release keeps serving; on Compose and the desktop a refusal changes nothing. A fresh install is not affected.

* **workflows:** an operator sets which findings count as insider leads, and who reads the cases they open (#4778, epic #4731). One `workflows.insider_leads` row in `system_config` (`{sources, rule_names, readers, teams}`, written through the config service so the writer is on the trail) is read at `GET /api/workflows/threat-hunt/insider-leads` (`automation.read`) and by the read-only `insider_leads` tool on `/mcp`, and written at `PUT` on the same path (`automation.write`); there is no write tool. No row, or no source or rule name, is off. A write that leaves it on needs a reader or team, each checked as for a restricted case: live people only, no service account, no default team, and teams need `users.read`. Adding a matcher, reader or team is a person's own act, not a token's; removing, or emptying the matchers, needs `automation.write` alone. A `restricted: true` start with no named case (HTTP or `start_run`) now opens its case to the starter plus the configured readers and teams; one deleted since is left off. The Workflows screen shows the list under the Threat Hunt workflow, read-only without `automation.write`. No migration; nothing starts a hunt from a match yet. Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **cases:** a Case's IOC list now says what each indicator does for intake and whether the index holds it yet (#4735, epic #4580). `GET /api/cases/{case_id}/iocs` and the `get_case_iocs` tool list the recorded IOCs, then the indicators its linked alerts name, from the one extractor (`core/storage/case_indicators.py`) through one `CaseIOCService.list_iocs`. Each row has `intake` (`attach`, `same_title`, `link` or `none`) and `linked` (false only for an indicator intake can use that the index does not hold yet, so a failed index write shows until the next successful one; null for a marked indicator or one intake does not use). A derived row keeps its role (`src_ip`, `dst_ip`, `ip`), hostnames are listed, and a row covered by a false-positive mark is reported as one. The Case's IOC card shows the label in each row's meta line and one line when any indicator is not linked yet. Computed on read; nothing is stored and there is no migration.

* **cases:** the shared IOC index now catches up on its own (#4734, epic #4580). A daemon loop (`_indicator_catchup_loop`, every `ORCHESTRATOR_LOOP_INTERVAL`, on every replica, whether or not AutoOps is on) reconciles a batch of up to 300 Cases per tick, each in its own short transaction claimed with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, so replicas split the work: Cases with `indicators_stale_since` set first (at most 100 per tick, oldest first), then Cases never synced, then the 25 reconciled longest ago, which repairs retention-deleted findings and any missed write. A Case that fails is logged and keeps (or gets) `indicators_stale_since`; it never stops the batch. Existing open Cases, hand-opened ones and those with an Investigation whose recorded IOCs had not counted, start receiving joined alerts as they are brought in, and the join notice makes this visible. Admins who hold `platform.write` see two live lines on the bell, computed per request and stored nowhere: "Bringing existing Cases in" while any Case has `indicators_synced_at` NULL, and "N Cases have indicators waiting past 15 minutes to be linked for intake" for Cases stale longer than 15 minutes (a never-synced Case does not count toward it). The legacy per-Investigation rows are retired in a later release once no Case remains unsynced, so an install that skips releases waits; rollback is restore-then-install (`docs/upgrading.md`). Revision `0094_case_indicator_catchup_idx` adds three partial indexes on `cases` (plain `CREATE INDEX`: it takes a moment on a large `cases` table, no rewrite and no row changes). Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **cases:** a Case is now the subject of the index intake joins alerts by (#4733, epic #4580). Every indicator a Case holds, with or without an Investigation, is indexed as it is added and removed as it leaves: a recorded IOC (API, MCP, bulk, sandbox report), a false-positive mark or its clearing, a finding linked or unlinked, a merge (both Cases), and a close. A hand-opened Case's source IPs and hashes now attach alerts at once, and a closed one is linked to. Only `src_ip`, `dst_ip`, `ip`, `hash` and `hostname` are indexed; other types stay on the Case alone. `source_ips`, `ips`, `ip_addresses` and `hashes` in an alert's `entity_context` now count, role-less. A failed index write no longer fails the change and is no longer dropped: it is logged, and `cases.indicators_stale_since` is set until the next write succeeds. Revision `0093_case_indicator_index` adds `shared_iocs.case_id` (nullable, `ON DELETE CASCADE`, unique with type and value), makes `shared_iocs.investigation_id` nullable, and adds `cases.indicators_synced_at` and `indicators_stale_since`. No row is copied or changed: a Case that exists at the upgrade keeps `indicators_synced_at` NULL, and matching keeps reading its Investigation rows until the first change to it reconciles it; Cases created later are reconciled from the start. The previous release still runs on the new schema (its rows keep their `investigation_id`). Nothing to do on Compose, Helm or desktop.

* **auth:** API tokens left unused are reminded, then revoked (#4725, epic #4722). `token_unused_days` on the session policy (default 45, 7-365, `null` is off; a longer period or off is a loosening, so a person's act) sets the period. Idle time counts from `last_used_at`, or creation if never used. An hourly pass (`services/api/token_sweep.py`, every replica, whatever the dormancy setting and the restore grace) sets `mcp_credentials.reminded_at` and tells the recipients once per pass, listing their tokens. A token is revoked, as a system act recorded as `credential.revoked` with reason `unused N days`, once its period is over and a counting reminder is at least 7 days old: sent after the last use, after the last restore settled, and no earlier than 7 days before the period's end. `authenticate` refuses such a token the same way, reading only stored state, so it stops on time if the pass is late; there is exactly one trail entry and one notice whichever gets there first. A rotated token in its grace, and directory sync's token while sync is on, are exempt. A policy that can't be read does nothing. Mint and rotate confirmations state the date an unused token would be revoked. Revision `0092_token_reminded_at` adds the column; the upgrade report counts tokens already idle past 45 days (`tokens_to_remind`), which are reminded at the first pass and revoked no sooner than 7 days later unless used (Compose and Helm alike; see `docs/upgrading.md`).

* **cases:** a person or agent that may record an IOC on a Case can now mark it a false positive, and matching honours it (#4681, epic #4580). `PUT /api/cases/{case_id}/iocs/false-positive` and the `set_case_ioc_false_positive` tool take an `ioc_type` and `value` and set or clear the mark; `cases.write` and being able to read the Case are the only checks. The mark is by folded value, so marking `dst_ip X` also covers `X` as `src_ip` or `ip`, and a value only a linked alert names gets a recorded, marked row. While marked, no alert joins the Case or links to it on that value; the Case still matches on its other values. Recording a marked value again, or a sandbox report naming it, stores it marked, and a merge carries the mark across. Each change is one `case.ioc.false_positive` or `case.ioc.false_positive.clear` trail entry with who made it. There is no migration. The STIX export still includes marked IOCs.

* **cases:** an alert that joins an open Case now tells the Case's assignee and watchers (#4682, epic #4580). The notice is in the bell (and mailed where mail is configured), reaches only people who may read the Case, and is not repeated for a person while their earlier join notice on that Case is unread. A watcher can switch it off with `alert_joined` in their notification preferences. A Case with no assignee and no watchers tells the active members of its team instead; a watcher who switched it off still counts as a watcher, so the team is not told. A finding a person removes from a Case (the API, the MCP tool or an update) is no longer joined back to that Case by intake, until it is linked to it again; intake then looks at the next matching Case or none. Revision `0091_case_finding_removals` adds the `case_finding_removals` table.

* **auth:** a username or email an erased person held is refused on the console and host routes too (#4585, epic #4575). Invite create and resend and service-account create tell an account manager it is "reserved", apart from "in use". An invitee choosing a username, first-run setup and a person changing their own email get one "in use or reserved" message, with no person or date. Case and full-width variants of a held address now hit the same refusal on these routes. A check on an account's own username or email is never refused by a reservation, so Medic's re-check of its own account still passes.

* **auth:** single sign-on and directory sync now refuse a username or email an erased person held (#4586, epic #4575). A first sign-in is refused (`sso:email_reserved` in the trail), an SSO email change keeps the old address and notes `email_reserved`, and a SCIM push is answered 409 `uniqueness` with "That username or email is in use or reserved" (it was "already in use"). A reserved push is never held in `directory_pending`, and no refusal record carries the address: SSO records the issuer and subject, SCIM the directory id and a `reason` of `reserved` or `in_use`. A SCIM attribute update now compares exactly, so a case-only change applies, and the userName filter and the returned `userName` match by `fold` (NFKC, then lowercase).

* **chat:** the Reasoning trace button and popup are gone from chat replies, and `GET /api/reasoning/{session_id}`, `GET /api/reasoning/{session_id}/interactions` and `GET /api/reasoning/{session_id}/interactions/{interaction_id}` are removed and now answer 404 (#4544). Nothing has written a `session_id` to `llm_interaction_logs` since v0.5.0, so the popup was empty for every conversation started since; the routes could only return older rows to anyone holding `automation.read`. Rows in `llm_interaction_logs` are kept, including older ones with a `session_id`, and still feed the cost and usage reports. The live Thinking text in a reply is unchanged.

* **auth:** Require single sign-on now stays in force while single sign-on can't be used, for example when its client secret can't be read from the secrets store, or the `auth.sso` row fails validation. Before, it quietly let password sign-in and reset links back in. Now only break-glass accounts sign in with a password until the client secret is saved again on the SSO card, or an operator runs `python -m core.auth.require_sso_off`, which also works on an invalid row. The backend logs an error once when this starts.

* **helm:** the agent shell now requires a bearer over TCP (#4570). `POST /run` without `Authorization: Bearer $SHELL_TOKEN` is answered 401, and the shell refuses to start over TCP with `SHELL_TOKEN` empty. The chart generates the token into a new `<fullname>-agent-shell` Secret (override with `secrets.agentShellToken`; set it under `helm template` or a GitOps controller) and gives it to the shell and the backend. With `secrets.existingSecret` or `secrets.externalSecret.enabled`, that Secret must carry a `SHELL_TOKEN` key: without it the shell pod does not become ready and `read_case_shell` reports unavailable. Compose and desktop use the shell's Unix socket and need nothing.
* **auth:** break-glass accounts (#4532). Revision `0081_break_glass` adds a nullable `users.break_glass_at`; no account is marked at the upgrade. `0086_join_break_glass` joins it with `0085_join_0084_heads`, the rest of the tree. Only the host marks or unmarks one: `scripts/reset_password.py --mark-break-glass <user>` / `--unmark-break-glass <user>`, with the script's operator prompts and trail entries (`user.break_glass_marked`, `user.break_glass_unmarked`). Marking refuses a service, inactive or deleted account, a password not on the current scheme, a role without `roles.manage`, live API tokens, and an identity-provider link, each with what to do instead. It replaces the account's recovery codes with 10 printed once, turns two-step on, ends its sign-ins, and warns when mail isn't configured. Under Require single sign-on a marked account signs in with its password, a second factor and a `reason` on `POST /api/auth/login` (the MFA-required 401 carries `break_glass: true`); an empty reason is refused before the code is checked. A recovery code is then a factor: that code is spent and nothing else changes. Each such sign-in is an `auth.break_glass_used` trail entry with the reason and one notice to each account manager. Its sign-in is `auth_method` `break_glass`, and "Confirm it's you" passes for its life. A marked account's lock is always timed (`lockout_minutes`), and it is left out of dormancy and password expiry. Under Require the confirm route accepts a marked account's host reset link and its own forced-change token; the host script issues it a link. The login page keeps "Sign in with SSO" and adds "Sign in with a password (break-glass accounts)".
* **agents:** agent-serve and agent-worker log in to Postgres as a new role, `vigil_agent`, instead of `vigil_app`. Revision `0081_agent_role` creates it and grants it only what the agent layer uses: `SELECT, INSERT` on `agent_events` and `agent_directives`, `SELECT, INSERT, UPDATE, DELETE` on `agent_run_leases`, and the `agent_directives` id sequence. A compromised agent container no longer holds a login that can write `users`, `sign_ins`, `roles`, `mcp_credentials` or any other table. The backend, daemon and workers stay on `vigil_app`. Its password is the new `POSTGRES_AGENT_PASSWORD`, which must differ from `POSTGRES_APP_PASSWORD` and the owner password; the upgrade step sets it as it sets `vigil_app`'s. **Compose:** add `POSTGRES_AGENT_PASSWORD` to `.env` (the default is `vigil_agent_password_change_me`, like the other published defaults). **Desktop:** minted per install, nothing to do. **Helm:** set `secrets.vigilAgentPassword` (unset, the chart generates one and keeps it across upgrades with `lookup`; under `helm template` or a GitOps controller, set it); with `secrets.existingSecret` or `secrets.externalSecret`, that Secret must also hold `POSTGRES_AGENT_PASSWORD`, or the chart's upgrade Job and agent pods do not start. The agent pods no longer receive `POSTGRES_APP_PASSWORD`. Where the database owner cannot `CREATE ROLE` (some managed databases), create `vigil_agent` (`LOGIN NOSUPERUSER`) before upgrading. `0083_merge_0081_heads` joins `0081_agent_role` with `0081_normalize_threat_indicators` and `0082_skills_approve`, which landed beside it.

* **cases:** teams' owner and membership rules now hold on three paths that missed them (#4453, epic #4306). `POST /api/cases/templates/{id}/create-case` with an `assignee` is refused whole, with no case made, as 400 `OWNER_CANNOT_WRITE` or `OWNER_OFF_TEAM` (judged against the default team), and a create that works now returns the case instead of a 500. A move may name yourself as owner without `cases.assign`; naming someone else, or replacing an owner who stays on the receiving team, still needs it. "On no team" counts only a live membership (a team that isn't archived, an active account): removal warnings and reactivation's join to the default team follow that, and a person whose only teams are archived is joined on reactivation.

* **cases:** teams added two refusals to the frozen case API in 1.1.2 (#4452, epic #4306). A newly set owner must hold `cases.write` and be a current member of the case's team. `PATCH /api/v1/cases/{id}` and the frozen `create_case` and `update_case` tools refuse otherwise: a person who does not exist or lacks `cases.write` is 400 `OWNER_CANNOT_WRITE`, and one who is not on the team is 400 `OWNER_OFF_TEAM`, including an inactive person whose membership row is still there. The tools return `{"error", "code"}` instead of a status, and `create_case` judges the default team. The `OWNER_OFF_TEAM` message points to `POST /api/v1/cases/{id}/move` and `move_case`. Echoing the stored owner and clearing it are not checked. A cross-team `POST /api/v1/cases/{id}/merge` (source and target on different teams) needs membership of the source team or `cases.move`, and otherwise returns 403 `Permission denied: cases.move required`. A same-team merge is unchanged, and there is no frozen MCP merge tool. `OWNER_CANNOT_WRITE` applies from 1.1.2, while everyone is still on the default team. The off-team refusal and the merge rule apply only once an admin splits teams. See [Upgrading](docs/upgrading.md#what-teams-add-to-the-case-api).

* **auth:** a recovery-code sign-in and an MFA reset end the other sign-ins as their own reasons, turning a detection off asks "Confirm it's you", and required MFA setup offers a second method (#4449). A sign-in ended by a recovery code is `recovery` ("Signed in with a recovery code"); one ended by an admin's MFA reset or `scripts/reset_password.py --reset-mfa` is `mfa_reset` ("Two-step sign-in was reset"). Both stop that sign-in's runs. The signed-out screen says so. A role, password, email or method change, and an admin ending sign-ins from the sign-in list, stay as they were. Revision `0079_recovery_mfa_reset` widens `sign_ins_end_reason_check` and leaves existing rows alone. `0080_merge_0079_heads` joins it with `0079_lockout_threshold`, which landed beside it. Reverting a live detection and removing a rule source ask before the handler runs; other detection writes do not, and `detections.write` is not a step-up permission. After a person newly covered by Require MFA saves their first method's codes, the setup offers the other method this install has (a security key or passkey after the app, the app after a key). Skip opens the console. Nothing extra is shown when there is no other method, on a voluntary setup, or for someone who already has MFA.

* **detections:** the daily detection gaps run (`agent-run-mitre_analyst`) no longer opens a Case. Each gap a rule can answer is a detection proposal (`GET /api/detection-engineering/proposals`), and a new blind spot is logged by the daemon (`new blind spots, no rule drafted`) and shown on the Detections page's Blind spots card; no run is queued for it. Cases the gaps run opened before the upgrade (titled from the gap, no alerts, no notes) are left as they are: close them by hand. A gaps run still queued at the upgrade, not yet launched, opens one more such Case ([#4490](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4490)).

* **auth:** failed sign-ins before a lock are 3–10 (`lockout_threshold`, default still 3). `PUT /api/session-policy` refuses any other value, and Settings › Sessions shows 3–10 because it reads the model's bounds. Revision `0079_lockout_threshold` moves a saved 1 or 2 to 3 and a saved 11–20 to 10, increments that document's version, and leaves `updated_by` unchanged. The move is one `session_policy.changed` trail entry, actor `system`, with the old and new documents. A saved 3–10, no saved policy, and a value outside 1–20 stay as stored, with no entry. The timed-lock length and the 100-failure cap are unchanged (#4450).

* **auth:** a person's own email change stays unconfirmed until they use a 24-hour link, and account notices name the account managers (#4445). `PUT /api/auth/me` mails that link when mail is configured and `VIGIL_FRONTEND_URL` is set, and otherwise says no link could be sent. The link opens `/confirm-email` and is used up by the confirm, not by opening the page; `POST /api/auth/email-confirmation` sends another. Until the address is confirmed, a self-service reset mails nothing (the reply is the same for every address, and says an unconfirmed change gets no link) and an emailed admin reset link needs `identity_confirmed_by`. The old address and the new one are both told of the change, and the person is told of their own password change, regenerated recovery codes, and turning off two-step sign-in or removing the authenticator app. Each of those says what changed, when, and the source address, and carries no link. Every notice to a person about their account or tokens ends with the other active people who hold `users.write`, named with their email, or keeps its previous wording when there is nobody else. Service accounts are never named. Revision `0077_email_confirmed` widens `auth_links.kind` with `email` and marks emails already on file confirmed. `0078_merge_0077_heads` joins it with `0077_directory_pending`, which landed beside it. New accounts, including invites and first-run setup, and an email the identity provider sets, are confirmed and get no link.

* **auth:** a first single sign-on links to an existing account by email only when the ID token carries `email_verified: true`. A token without the claim still signs in an account already linked to its subject, and still creates a new account for an address no account holds. A provider that omits the claim (Microsoft Entra ID often does) is refused at the login page with "email not verified" for a person who already has a local account. The trail records `sso:email_link_unverified`. A self-service password reset no longer issues or honours a link for an account that has no password and is linked to a provider. An administrator's reset link or the host script can still give that account a password.

* **auth:** unlocking an account and issuing a password reset link now take a typed reason and end that person's open sign-ins (#4439). `POST /api/users/{id}/unlock` and `POST /api/users/{id}/reset-link` refuse a missing or blank `reason` (500 characters at most) and keep it as `note` on `user.unlocked` and `reset.sent`. Each open sign-in ends as `ended_by_admin`, with the same text on `auth.sign_in_ended`. The person and the other holders of `users.write` are told the sign-ins were ended. A lock still ends no sign-in by itself. Who may run each act, how a person is confirmed, the step-up on the reset link, and "Suspected compromise" are unchanged. A person's own reset, `scripts/reset_password.py`, and the MFA reset are unchanged.

* **medic:** on Compose, `scripts/medic/enable-compose.sh` mints the gateway's token (#4429, epic #4425). After the backend is up it runs `python -m core.auth.service_account token <name>` with its output going straight into `<secrets dir>/medic_gateway_token` (`0600`, uid 10002 on Linux), then recreates `medic-gateway`, which mounts it as the new `medic_gateway_token` secret. A fresh enable makes no `viewer_password`. A plain re-run with a token file mints nothing; `--rotate` rotates the token (the old one works for 24 hours) as well as the API key; `--secrets-only` mints nothing and says so. An existing `viewer_password` is left in place and never rotated. `scripts/lib.sh`'s `dc` creates an empty `0600` placeholder for a missing token or password file, so `start.sh` starts the gateway with either missing, and a site enabled before keeps reading on its password with no step. Re-running the script moves it; the password ends on the token's first use. The password path is removed no earlier than the release that ends the announced window. After a restore from before the mint, delete `medic_gateway_token` and re-run the script.

* **auth:** the roles screen lists new-capability permissions no custom or legacy role holds, and marks `audit.forward` and `audit.manage` as "no effect for a service account" wherever a role shows them granted. `GET /api/users/roles/list` returns `new_permissions_unheld` and `person_only`. The permission-split upgrade report (`0008_permission_catalog`) now says whether any active person can manage roles afterwards — "Nobody on this install can manage roles" plus `python -m core.auth.recover_admin` when none can, otherwise that someone can — and states that approving through chat or a person's MCP token no longer approves. What the revision grants is unchanged ([#4433](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4433)).

* **agents:** the upgrade ends unfinished runs whose stored principal can't carry them under run scoping (#4348, epic #4345). Revision `0065_end_unscoped_runs` judges each `running` or `paused` `workflow_runs` row that has a ledger, by the principal on its `run` event, read without verifying the signature. When it is missing, can't be decoded, names another run or has expired, the run is ended in the migration's transaction: its row is finalised as failed with `started before run scoping; run it again` (in `error`, `outcome` and `reason`), its ledger gets a `terminal` with outcome `failed` and that reason (none is added when one exists), and its pending approvals are rejected by `system` with that reason, each with a case-trail entry. Queued runs (no ledger) and child runs are left alone; Redis and the job queue are not touched. The upgrade report counts them as `ended_runs`. No schema change. `docs/upgrading.md` gains "What run scoping does to unfinished runs on upgrade day", including rollback by install shape.

* **auth:** a lost or exposed pepper: emergency rotation and restore leave people able to reset, never locked in a loop (#4299, epic #4164). `python -m core.auth.key_ring emergency pepper` (and the `pepper` in `emergency all`) now retires every earlier pepper key and re-encrypts nothing, so every stored password and recovery-code hash becomes unreadable; `rotate pepper` and `emergency mfa` are unchanged. A stored password that starts with `pepper-` and names a version no live key holds is the forced-reset state: no column, no migration. Signing in with it is refused with a 403 (`password_reset_required`) that says the password must be reset, adds nothing to the failed count and sets no lock, and is recorded on the trail as `auth.sign_in_failed` with that reason; the sign-in screen offers Forgot password, or says to ask an administrator when mail is off. A reset (link, `scripts/reset_password.py`) works without reading the old hash and is never held back by the minimum password age; it skips history entries it cannot open (counted as `history_skipped` in `auth.password_reset`) and removes recovery codes it cannot open, leaving the authenticator app and security keys alone. A restore counts the people whose password names a pepper version no live key holds and carries that count (`passwords_to_reset`) in its one notice and its trail entry. The emergency notice and trail entry carry the count too. Docs: `docs/compromise-recovery.md` (4a). Replaces the 1.1.2 caveat that a restore whose ring cannot be opened leaves every password unusable.

* **auth:** the pepper: stored password and recovery-code hashes are encrypted under a key-ring key (#4298, epic #4164). A new encrypting purpose, `pepper`, gets a random key at the first start. Each PBKDF2 hash (`users.password_hash`, every `password_history` entry, every `mfa_recovery_codes` entry) is stored as `pepper-<version>$<Fernet token>` around the PBKDF2 string, so a database copy without the ring can check no password. The first start wraps every existing PBKDF2 hash once, under the advisory lock, and counts them in the `auth.keys_adopted` trail entry as `password_hashes`; later starts wrap only a bare hash an older replica wrote meanwhile. bcrypt hashes and empty ones (people who sign in only through single sign-on) are left as they are, and a bcrypt hash is still replaced by a wrapped PBKDF2 one at the person's next good sign-in. `python -m core.auth.key_ring rotate pepper` is added to the host command: it moves every hash under the new key in one transaction, keeps the old key decrypt-only (never retired), and records the count as `password_hashes` in `auth.key_rotated`; `rotate mfa` no longer touches hashes and `rotate pepper` leaves MFA secrets alone. A ring that cannot be read now refuses a sign-in with a 503 instead of counting a wrong password, so no failure or lock moves; a wrapped hash no key opens never matches. Until #4299 lands, a restore whose ring cannot be opened leaves every password unusable. Downgrading is not supported once hashes are wrapped: older code cannot read them.

* **auth:** the sign-in upgrade is closed out (#4297, epic #4164). `JWT_ACCESS_EXPIRATION_MINUTES`, `JWT_REFRESH_EXPIRATION_DAYS`, `AUTH_MIN_ZXCVBN_SCORE` and `PASSWORD_RESET_TTL_SECONDS` are removed from `core/config.py`, `env.example` and the Helm values and fixed at 30 minutes, 7 days, score 3 and 3600 seconds; a value still set in the environment or `.env` is ignored, and the API logs one line per variable at start. Delete them from a copied `.env`. Revision `0061_users_cutoff_drop` drops `users.tokens_valid_after`, unread since `0042`; adopted 0.7.0 and 0.8.0 databases get it from `0004` and lose it here, as a fresh install does. `docs/upgrading.md` gains "What the sign-in changes do on upgrade day": every sign-in ends once, dormancy clocks start at the upgrade, failure counts reset, old recovery codes are voided with one accepted once, the order of the first sign-in, the settings that left, and how to read `auth.password_scheme_upgraded_at`, which the 12 months for the old password hashing count from.

* **audit:** the erasure list can live apart from the upgrade directory, and is written safely from several replicas (#4293, epic #4275). `VIGIL_ERASURE_DIR` holds `erased-principals.txt`; unset, it stays in `VIGIL_UPGRADE_DIR`, so existing installs change nothing. Each append or rewrite takes a Postgres advisory lock and is fsynced. A due deferred erasure now waits when the list can't be written, and `GET /api/audit/retention` returns `blocked` (the reason, or null), shown in Settings > Audit.

* **auth:** keys: backup and restore, emergency rotation, overdue rotation (#4181, epic #4164). Revision `0060_auth_keys_wrapped_at` adds `auth_keys.wrapped_at` (backfilled from `created_at`), the wrapping secret's age. Each backup now writes a second restic snapshot in the same repository, tagged `wrapping-key` and `main-<id>`, holding only `JWT_SECRET_KEY`, and the snapshot's `.env` loses that line (restore keeps the live file's; older snapshots still carry theirs). `restore --snapshot latest` skips wrapping-key snapshots, and retention forgets those whose backup is gone. A restore opens the restored key ring under the install's secret, then the paired one (re-wrapping it), and otherwise replaces it instead of failing: no old token verifies, authenticator secrets no live key opens are cleared (`mfa_secret`, `mfa_recovery_codes`, `mfa_last_totp_step`; `mfa_enabled` only for people with no security key), and the one `restore_settled` notice gains a sentence with the count (`apps_cleared` joins the trail entry). Versions retired before the restore stay retired and their numbers are never reused (`rotate` now writes max version + 1). `python -m core.auth.key_ring` gains `emergency <purpose|all>` (no overlap; stops every run through `stop_run`, "emergency key change"; one notice to the holders of `sessions.manage`, listed by the new `permissions.holders`), `inventory [--json]` (every key, retired too, no material), `rewrap` and `reset-mfa --yes`. A background task rotates any key older than 12 months with its normal overlap; a failure keeps the old key and shows a bell condition to `sessions.manage` holders until one succeeds. A `JWT_SECRET_KEY` from `secrets.enc` rotates the same way; one set outside Vigil gets a trail entry 30 days before 12 months and a bell condition once overdue. New trail actions `auth.key_ring_reset`, `auth.key_ring_rewrapped`, `auth.wrapping_key_rotated`, `auth.wrapping_key_due`, `auth.wrapping_key_overdue`, `auth.key_rotation_failed`, `auth.key_rotation_recovered`, `auth.emergency_rotation`, `auth.apps_cleared_all`. New `docs/compromise-recovery.md`.

* **auth:** erasure blanks the sign-in details, and catches up earlier erasures (#4182, epic #4164). `retention._blank_beside` now sets `sign_ins.user_agent`, `device` and `address` to NULL for the erased principal, so the erasure path and the post-restore `reapply` both do it; the rows, their ids and the `erased` end reason stay. Revision `0059_erased_sign_ins` blanks the same three columns, in one `UPDATE`, for every principal already in `$VIGIL_UPGRADE_DIR/erased-principals.txt` (nothing when it is unset or empty; the report counts the ids). There is no table of failed attempts to blank; a failed attempt's address is in `audit_trail.source_ip`, which erasure already blanks.

* **cases:** filter the Cases list by who holds cases, or Unassigned (#4266). New `GET /api/v1/cases/owners` (`cases.read`) lists each distinct non-blank owner stored on a case the reader can list, closed included, with its `owner_state`. `GET /api/v1/cases` takes `unassigned=true` and `POST /api/v1/cases/search` takes `"unassigned": true`, matching cases whose owner is NULL or `""`, alongside any named `assignee`. The console's Assigned dropdown, Filters panel and advanced search offer those owners (stale ones labelled "not a current account") plus Unassigned, and case rows, the case page and task rows carry the label. `?unassigned=1` opens the list on Unassigned.

* **cases:** pick colleagues from a people list (#4265, epic #4262). New `GET /api/users/people` returns every active, non-service, non-deleted person as `username` and `display_name` (null when empty), nothing else; it needs a person's own sign-in holding `cases.assign` or `cases.write`. An API token or a service account's sign-in gets 401 `A person sign-in is required`, an MCP credential the existing refusal, and anyone else without either permission a 403. `require_person_permission` takes further either-of permissions and `refuse_machine`. The console's Edit case owner, Add task, each existing task row and Reopen now pick from that list (or Unassigned, a real empty value); a stored owner that isn't current shows as stored, labelled "not a current account", and is never offered. When a save is refused with `OWNER_NOT_CURRENT`, the same step offers the picker to whoever may change that owner, or says that someone holding `cases.assign` must reassign it. A task counts as done when `completed` or `cancelled`. If the list can't load, the picker takes a typed username or email. Types regenerated. No migration.

* **cases:** open work needs a current owner (#4263). Revision `0057_current_owners` rewrites each `cases.assignee` and `case_tasks.assignee` that names exactly one non-service account, by username or email ignoring case and in any account state, to that account's username; a row older than the account (`principals.created_at` after the row's `updated_at`) is skipped. No trail entry is written and the upgrade report gives the count per table (`rewritten_owners`). A stored owner is current only when it is exactly the username of an active, non-service person whose account is not deleted. A case update (`PATCH /api/v1/cases/{id}`, the agents' and MCP `update_case`) that leaves the case not `closed`, and a task update (`PUT /api/cases/{id}/tasks/{task_id}`, MCP `update_case_task`) that leaves the task not `completed` or `cancelled`, is refused with 400 `OWNER_NOT_CURRENT` when its resulting owner is not current, whether or not it sent one; setting a current person or `""` (Unassigned) in the same update saves, and closing, completing or cancelling saves. Agent and MCP tools return `{"error", "code"}`. Case reads (`GET /api/v1/cases`, `GET /api/v1/cases/{id}`, `POST /api/v1/cases/search`, MCP `get_case` and `list_cases`), task reads (`GET /api/cases/{id}/tasks`, MCP `get_case_tasks`) and the case view's `case.json` and `tasks.json` carry `owner_state`: `current`, `not_current`, or null when unassigned. The deactivation notice counts a task as open unless it is `completed` or `cancelled`.

* **auth:** a sign-in that ends another under the sign-in limit tells the person, and two in an hour tell managers (#4242, epic #4164). Login sends one `sign_in_limit` notice per evicting login, after the commit, whatever number of sign-ins it ended: it names the new sign-in's id in its metadata and the stored `message` is generic; the bell composes the browser, device and address from the `sign_ins` row when it renders (so erasure that blanks the row blanks the notice), and `notify()` takes a `mail_body` so the mailed copy, outside erasure, says where it came from. The notice links to `/overview?sign_ins=1`, which opens the person's sign-in list. The managers (`auth_service.managers`) get one `sign_in_limit_repeat` notice, no addresses, linking to `/settings?section=users`, when the login makes the account's count of distinct `sign_in_limit` endings in the last hour exactly 2; a third and later in the hour tell no one. Derived from `sign_ins` rows; no counter, table or migration; service accounts have no limit and are never evicted.

* **backup:** a restore now settles the people it brings back (#4241, epic #4164). After the key rotation, in its own step, it ends every open sign-in with the new reason `restore` (listed as "Ended by a restore"; revision `0056_restore_end_reason` widens `sign_ins_end_reason_check`), stops every run the restored database shows as `running` or `paused` (with its children; `stopped: sign-in ended`, through `sign_in_runs._stop`, plus the ledger terminal at once, because the host command has no workers and `stop_run`'s escalation timer dies with it), and starts a 7-day dormancy grace: the sweep deactivates nobody and starts no warnings until `auth.restore_settled_at` (a `system_config` row; `dormancy.grace_until()` / `in_grace()`) plus 7 days, then warns first as before. Each holder of `sessions.manage` gets one `restore_settled` notice with the counts (sign-ins already ended `erased` by the restore's reapply are counted), the stale clocks and the lost locks; no per-person notice. The restore's trail entry and output gain `sign_ins_ended` and `runs_stopped`. Last-sign-in dates, failure counts and locks return as backed up.

* **auth:** require phishing-resistant MFA (#4236). New session-policy field `require_phishing_resistant` (`nobody`, `privileged`, `everyone`; default `nobody`, so old rows load unchanged), never above `require_mfa` (a 422 naming both fields; nothing is adjusted). `session_policy.mfa_covered` stays the one coverage check and now takes either level (`nobody` covers no one); lowering the level is a loosening. Raising it is a person's own act that needs the person to hold a key and keys to be available: a machine caller gets a 403 and a trail entry, a person without a key a 403, and unavailable keys a 409 with the reason naming the hostname. The dry run and the saved response carry `without_key`, the covered active people with no key (`mfa_coverage.without_key`), and the save adds `notice`. The forced-setup gate in `_check_sign_in` (`current_user.setup_needed`, also behind `mfa_setup_required` on login and `/me`) now holds a covered person with no key while keys are available; the app does not count: an app code from a covered person with no key signs in but opens no privileged window, and from one with a key is refused. While keys are unavailable the app counts, and the sign-in row's `auth_method` is `mfa_step_down` with `step_down` on the `auth.sign_in` entry; `sessions.manage` holders see a live Condition. A key-only person's recovery code now verifies at login; with keys unavailable and the requirement covering them it is spent without the recovery of #4233 (`auth_method` `recovery_step_down`, `auth.recovery_code_used`, the person and managers told) and that sign-in's window lets them add the app and make fresh codes. Sessions screen: the three-option control and the count in the confirm dialog. Types regenerated. No migration ([#4236](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4236)).

* **auth:** security keys, the console (#4235). The sign-in screen asks for a key when the login answer carries one (the prompt starts straight after the password; a person with a key and the app chooses either, and *Use a recovery code* stays for those with the app); a cancelled or timed-out prompt sends nothing and counts nothing. The two-step dialog lists a person's keys, adds one (named, "Your admin can see this name") and removes one, and its regenerate, remove-app and add-key checks take a key touch in place of a code; a person with no MFA adds a first key with the password alone and gets recovery codes. *Confirm it's you* offers the key path (a waiting panel for one factor). When keys are off the screens show no key controls and a *Sign-in methods* line with the server's reason and a link to the new [docs/security-keys.md](docs/security-keys.md). The browser's own `PublicKeyCredential.parse*OptionsFromJSON` and `toJSON()` carry the options; no new dependency, no server change ([#4235](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4235)).

* **auth:** security keys, the server half (#4234). A person registers WebAuthn keys, lists and removes them under `/api/auth/mfa/keys` (`GET`, `POST /options`, `POST`, `POST /assertion-options`, `DELETE /{key_id}`), and signs in with one: `POST /api/auth/login` answers the password with the existing 401 and `X-MFA-Required`, now with `methods` and, for a person with keys, `security_key` (request options and a signed `challenge`); the second call repeats the password and adds `webauthn` and `webauthn_challenge`. A person with keys and the app may use either, and `POST /api/auth/confirm` and every method change take `assertion` and `challenge` in place of `code` (the challenge comes from `POST /api/auth/mfa/keys/assertion-options`). A wrong assertion counts toward the lockout as a wrong code does; a replay is refused by the signature counter and the stored last challenge. Library: py_webauthn (`webauthn`, BSD-3-Clause), added to `requirements.txt` and the lock; attestation is "none" and is not checked. Only FIPS 186-5 algorithms are offered and accepted (ES256, ES512, Ed25519, RSA PKCS1 and PSS with SHA-2 on 2048 bits or more); ES384 is in the standard but the library cannot verify it. New setting `VIGIL_CONSOLE_HOSTNAME` (`env.example`, Helm `config`) is the relying-party id, never the request's `Host`: keys are available when it is set and `VIGIL_COOKIE_SECURE` is on, or when it is `localhost`; otherwise registration and the sign-in challenge are refused with a reason naming the hostname, which `GET /api/auth/me` and login carry as `user.security_keys`. A key-only person cannot sign in while keys are unavailable. Revision `0054_security_keys` adds `security_keys`. `users.mfa_enabled` means "has at least one method": the first key of a person without MFA turns it on and returns recovery codes once (not usable at login for a key-only person yet); the app can be set up beside keys (`mfa_methods.has_app`) and `DELETE /api/auth/mfa` removes only the app when keys remain; removing the last key of a key-only person turns MFA off, and removing the last method of a person the requirement covers is refused (409). Removing a key ends the person's other sign-ins (`account_changed`); adding one ends none and notifies them; a recovery code at login removes the keys too. Registering, removing and the app's removal are trail entries. The key ring gains the `webauthn` purpose; on an install already adopted, start-up adds a current key for any purpose the ring lacks (`auth.keys_adopted`). Not in this change: the console, the phishing-resistant requirement and recovery codes at login for a key-only person ([#4235](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4235), [#4236](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4236); [#4234](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4234)).

* **auth:** an administrator can import a newer breached-password list offline (#4175). `POST /api/session-policy/breached-list` (`sessions.manage`; a machine caller may use it, as an import only adds, and it asks no "Confirm it's you") takes a plain-text file, one entry per line, up to 64 MB, read as a stream. Entries under 12 characters, over 128, email-shaped (the regex in `data/NOTICE`), not UTF-8 or blank are dropped; the rest are lowercased and added to the new `breached_passwords` table (revision `0055_breached_passwords`; the app role may read and insert, nothing deletes). The answer counts lines read, added, already listed (shipped, imported before or repeated) and skipped, and the trail's `breached_list.imported` entry carries those counts and the file's SHA-256, never an entry. Every accepted import counts as a refresh, even one that adds nothing; the last one is the `system_config` row `auth.breached_list`. `GET` on the same path gives the shipped list's date and size, the imported count and the last refresh. Passwords are refused, and a sign-in is sent to change, when they are in the shipped file or the table; there is still no online lookup. `data/NOTICE` now has a `Checked: YYYY-MM-DD` line (update it with the file at each release). A list whose later of that date and the last import is over 90 days old is a live warning in the bell for `sessions.manage` holders and, once per stale period, an `auth.breached_list_stale` trail entry written by an hourly daemon check under an advisory lock. Settings › Sessions has a Breached passwords card with the date, counts and an import control. The import is the operator's choice and the operator's licence. Not in this change: an online breach service ([#4175](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4175)).

* **auth:** changing your own sign-in methods asks again, and recovery removes every method. `POST /api/auth/mfa/recovery-codes` and `DELETE /api/auth/mfa` take `password` and `code` and run the strong check (`step_up.confirm(..., strong=True)`) on every call, inside the privileged window too, and no longer spend the code a second time; `POST /api/auth/mfa/verify` takes the password for the first method (and no code beyond the first authenticator code), and turning MFA on again (an earlier `auth.mfa_enabled` or `auth.mfa_disabled` in the trail) notifies the person. A wrong answer counts toward the three that end the sign-in. New codes and MFA off end the person's other sign-ins with `account_changed` (stopping their runs) and leave the acting one open: `sign_ins.end_all_for_user` takes `except_sign_in_id`. A current-scheme recovery code accepted at `POST /api/auth/login` (`AuthService.verify_mfa_code` now returns which kind was accepted, still truthy) removes the app, its steps and the remaining codes, ends every other sign-in, notifies the person and writes `auth.mfa_recovered`; the response carries `mfa_removed` and the console opens setup. Old-scheme codes still only upgrade the set (#4177); a locked account is refused before any code is checked. `GET /api/auth/mfa-methods` lists your methods and `GET /api/users/{id}/mfa-methods` (`users.write`, role covered) a person's: type, name, added (from the trail), last used (from the app's last accepted step) and recovery codes left, never a secret or a code. The console's sign-in form has *Use a recovery code*, the two-step dialog asks for the password, and the Users tab has a read-only *Sign-in methods* dialog. No migration ([#4233](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4233)).

* **auth:** the minimum password length now goes down to 12 (`password_min_length`, 12-64) and password age joins the session policy. Under 15 needs `require_mfa` to be `everyone`: a `PUT /api/session-policy` that breaks the link, whichever field moved, is a 422 naming both fields, and nothing is adjusted silently. The Sessions screen's minimum is 12 and its hint says so. `password_max_age_days` (1-365, `null` off by default) and `password_min_age_days` (0-7, 0 off) are new fields with defaults, so old rows and bodies still load, and no migration is needed. `is_loosening` counts a longer or removed maximum and a lower minimum age; any change to the maximum, tightening too, needs a person's own sign-in (a service account's `PUT` gets a 403 and a trail entry), while a machine may raise the minimum age. A password older than the maximum (counted from `password_changed_at`, else `created_at`) is a forced change at sign-in after the MFA check: `password_change_required` and a reset token, the same path as a listed or short password; service accounts are never blocked. Within 7 days of expiry the bell shows "Your password expires in N days", which opens the change-password dialog; holders of `users.write` see the service accounts past the maximum until their `password_changed_at` is fresh. Both are live conditions, never stored. The minimum age refuses only a person's voluntary change, with a 400 (`code` `password_min_age`) saying when it ends: `/api/auth/change-password`, and `/api/auth/password-reset/confirm` unless the token is the one sign-in issued for a forced change (it now carries `forced`) or the account is locked. The minimum age must be below the maximum when one is set (a 422 names both fields). Administrator resets, `scripts/reset_password.py` (its link is minted `forced`) and service-account rotation never check it, and every exempt change still enters history. `POST /api/auth/change-password` takes `suspected_leak`: it waives the minimum age, is recorded on the `auth.password_changed` entry (`suspected_leak`, `min_age_waived`) and notifies the holders of `users.write`; the dialog offers it only after a minimum-age refusal. Not in this change, and still to come on [#4175](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4175): the offline breached-list import and the stale-list warning.

* **auth:** API tokens reach the HTTP API (#4223). New tokens are `vgl_api_...` (SHA-256 in `mcp_credentials`, as before); `vgl_mcp_` tokens stay MCP-only and are still refused on HTTP with the same message, and `authenticate` takes both. An `Authorization: Bearer vgl_api_...` is judged alone, even with a cookie beside it, and every failure (unknown, revoked, expired, inactive holder) is the same 401. A token is a machine caller whoever holds it: `vigil_http_actor` has `via="token"` and `person=false`, so it never widens access, never meets step-up, is refused on every `/api/auth/*` route (403), cannot mint or rotate, and passes the audit poll only for a service account holding `audit.read` (a person still needs a sign-in plus `audit.forward`). It also skips the CSRF check, as a header credential has nothing for it to protect. Any active person mints their own at `POST /api/tokens` (no `integrations.write`): 90 days by default, 365 at most, 365 for a service account (one function, `mcp_credential_service.lifetime`), no "never". `POST /api/tokens/{id}/rotate` mints a successor with the same holder and label (`mcp_credentials.successor_id`) and ends the old token at the earlier of its own expiry and 24 hours from now, or now with `suspected_compromise`; a rotated, revoked or expired token answers 409, and only whoever could mint for the holder may rotate. A `users.write` holder lists every token (`GET /api/tokens/all`, filtered by `holder` and `status` on the server) and revokes any, including one held by someone with more access, which is the one exception to the privileges-cover-target rule; a service account holding `users.write` may revoke too. Mint, rotate and revoke are trail entries and tell the holder; a token expiring within 14 days is a live warning to its holder, and for a service account's token to every `users.write` holder. Revision `0052_api_tokens` adds `successor_id` and moves every unrevoked token with no expiry, or more than 365 days left, to 90 days from the upgrade (one `credential.expiry_set` trail entry each; a second run changes nothing). The console's Settings › System › Users has a Tokens section for everyone and an all-tokens list for `users.write`; the MCP panel lost its credential list and `GET /api/mcp/surface` its `credentials` field (it answers `has_token`), and `POST /api/mcp/surface/credentials` and `DELETE /api/mcp/surface/credentials/{id}` are gone. Not in this change: a service account's mint route, per-token scopes, unused-token revocation ([#4223](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4223)).

* **auth:** privileged changes ask "Confirm it's you" unless the sign-in passed MFA inside the privileged idle window (`idle_privileged_minutes`, default 10). The window is `sign_ins.confirmed_at` (revision `0050_step_up`, which also adds `confirm_failures`, widens `end_reason` to 30 characters and adds the end reason `too_many_confirmations`): completing MFA at sign-in, finishing MFA setup, or `POST /api/auth/confirm` (a current authenticator or recovery code, or the password for an account without MFA; `strong: true` takes the password and a code) starts or restarts it and clears the failure count. A password-only sign-in starts none. Which writes ask is `STEP_UP` in `core/auth/permissions.py` (`platform.write`, `integrations.write`, `integrations.install`, `ai_autonomy.write`, `sessions.manage`, `roles.manage`, `users.write`, `users.delete`); `deny_unless` asks by itself on a non-read call, so `require_permission` and inline checks are covered alike; user and role writes and minting an MCP credential also add `step_up.gate`, because they record their own 403s. `ai_decisions.approve` never asks. Agent runs, service accounts and every non-person caller are never asked. The refusal is a 403 with `detail.code` `confirmation_required` (`variant`, `action`, `window_minutes`, `confirmed_at`), or `step_up_mfa_setup` for a person with no MFA, who cannot make these changes, minting a personal MCP credential included, until they set it up; finishing setup starts the window.
