The console shows the dialog from `api.ts`, then retries the request. Three failed confirmations in a row (`/api/auth/confirm`, the password-and-code check on email change, and ending your own sign-ins) end the sign-in with `too_many_confirmations`, stop its runs (it is in `STOPS_RUNS`), answer 401 and never lock the account; each success and failure is an audit entry. Changing your email on `PUT /api/auth/me` now always takes the strong check, inside the window too: `current_password` and, with MFA, a new `code` field. Not in this change: a security-key variant of the dialog ([#4178](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4178)), a step-up on disabling a detection (no route turns one off today), and ending other people's sign-ins. Ending your own sign-ins counts failures but still asks every time ([#4179](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4179)).

* **auth:** `require_mfa` (`off`, `privileged`, `everyone`; default `privileged`) joins the session policy, so old rows take the default and nothing migrates. "Privileged" is any role holding a permission in `PRIVILEGED_PERMISSIONS`, judged by permissions held; service accounts are never covered (`session_policy.mfa_covered` is the one check). A covered, active person with no MFA can sign in with the password but every route except `POST /api/auth/mfa/setup`, `POST /api/auth/mfa/verify`, `GET /api/auth/me`, refresh and logout answers 403 with `detail.code` `mfa_setup_required`. The check runs on every request (`_check_sign_in`), so a role edit or a raised setting applies at the person's next request, and a script's Bearer sign-in with no MFA is held too; `DEV_MODE` is unchanged. `GET /api/auth/me` and login carry `mfa_setup_required`; the console shows a setup step (the two-step dialog's setup, then the recovery codes) before anything else. Lowering the level counts as loosening in `is_loosening`, so a service account's `PUT /api/session-policy` that does it is refused and trailed. While the level is below `everyone`, holders of `sessions.manage` see "N people can sign in without MFA" in the bell's Conditions, and the daemon's scheduler writes one `auth.mfa_coverage_gap` entry a day (actor `system`) with the count, checking the trail for today's entry first. The daemon runs under `--profile daemon`, so the daily entry is written only when it is up ([#4177](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4177)).

* **auth:** an account with no sign-in and no personal MCP token use for `dormancy_days` (session policy, default 35, 7-90, `null` is off) is deactivated by an hourly sweep. Its activity is the latest of `users.last_login`, its credentials' `last_used_at`, `created_at` and the new `users.dormancy_floor`; a failed sign-in, a lock and an agent run's token are not activity, and no per-request write is added. Revision `0048_dormancy` sets the floor to the upgrade, so nobody is deactivated for time before it, and adds `dormancy_floor`, `dormancy_warned_at` and `dormancy_held_at`. The holder and every holder of `users.write` get a warning once per idle stretch, 7 days before (less if the period is shorter), listing the agent runs in flight; an account is never deactivated before a warning has been out for that long, even when the period is shortened. Service accounts are never deactivated, and neither is the last active person holding `roles.manage`: managers get one high-priority notice instead. Deactivation (`user.deactivated_dormant`, actor `system`) revokes the account's MCP credentials and ends its sign-ins, which stops their runs; when no person at a screen did it, managers also get one notice with the count of the account's open cases (linked to `/cases?owner=`) and open tasks. Removing or disabling an account (`DELETE` or `PUT {"is_active": false}`) now goes through the same `deactivate_account`, so a disable revokes the credentials too. Reactivating an account restarts its clock and clears any lock. Changing `dormancy_days` at all, tightening included, needs a person's own sign-in: a service account's `PUT /api/session-policy` that changes it is refused and trailed, and `is_loosening` counts a longer period or turning it off. The Users tab marks an account inside its warning window "Deactivating soon" (`deactivating_at` in the user payload); Cases opens filtered to one assignee with `?owner=`. Lockout is in [#4211](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4211) ([#4176](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4176)).

* **auth:** lockout is now part of the session policy (`auth.session_policy`), read at every failure, and `AUTH_LOCKOUT_THRESHOLD` / `AUTH_LOCKOUT_DURATION_MINUTES` are removed from `core/config.py`, `env.example` and the Helm values (ignored if set). `lockout_threshold` is 3 failures in a row (3-10). A lock lasts until an administrator's Unlock or the person's password reset link; `lockout_timed: true` makes it end after `lockout_minutes` (30-1440), though 100 failures in a row still lock until unlocked. `PUT /api/session-policy` counts a higher threshold, switching to timed mode, or a shorter timed lock as loosening, so a service account may not make them. Only a password failure, or a wrong code at the sign-in MFA step, counts; a wrong code when enabling or disabling MFA, regenerating recovery codes or confirming an action no longer does, and only a complete sign-in (or an Unlock, or a reset) resets the count, so those two MFA calls no longer reset it. A lock ends no sign-in. When it is set, every holder of `users.write` gets a notice, and the person gets one with when and from where (`users.locked_at`, `users.locked_from`, revision `0047_lockout_policy`, which also resets every failure count). The 423 no longer carries `Retry-After` for a lock until unlocked; its `detail` says what ends it, and that a reset link cannot be mailed when mail is not set up, and `locked_until` is null. `POST /api/users/{id}/unlock` needs `users.write`, a person's own sign-in and a role the caller covers (not your own account), takes `identity_confirmed_by` (`in_person`, `video_call`, `phone_call_back`, `other_verified_channel`) and records it in the audit trail as `user.unlocked`. The Users tab shows who is locked and has an Unlock control. Dormancy is not in this change ([#4176](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4176)).

* **auth:** minimum password length, character rules and history are now fields of the session policy document (`/api/session-policy`, same `If-Match` and machine tighten-only rules): `password_min_length` (default 16, 15–64 until Require MFA, #4177, allows 12), `password_character_rules` (`none`, `letters_digits`, `four_classes`, counted by Unicode category) and `password_history` (default 5, 4–24). Every route that sets a password applies them, and the refusal names the minimum or the missing class. A person without two-step sign-in whose password is under 15 characters gets the same `password_change_required` answer and reset token at sign-in as a listed password; with two-step sign-in a 12–14 character password is kept until their next change. `AUTH_MIN_PASSWORD_LENGTH` and `AUTH_PASSWORD_HISTORY_LIMIT` are removed from the settings, `env.example` and the Helm values: delete them from a copied `.env`. A value other than the old default (12, 5) is carried once into the policy when it has no saved version (moved into range, recorded "carried from host configuration"), and the variable is never read again; a value equal to the old default gets the new default. Checking a password against 24 earlier hashes now derives them in parallel. The console's "At least 12 characters" hints are gone; the server's refusal states the minimum. Age, the offline list import and the stale-list warning are later changes ([#4175](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4175)).

* **auth:** Settings › Sessions edits the session policy for holders of `sessions.manage`: a save sends the version in `If-Match`, a stale one shows what is saved now and who changed it and saves nothing, and a change the server counts as loosening asks first (`PUT /api/session-policy?dry_run=true` answers `{"loosening": bool}` and saves nothing; `PolicyOut` gains `updated_by_name`). The console shows a countdown for the last two minutes before a sign-in goes idle, shared across tabs, cleared by any action in any tab or *Stay signed in*, and a note five minutes before the sign-in's length limit. `GET /api/auth/me` gains `sign_in` (`idle_in_seconds`, `ends_in_seconds`). A 401 for an ended sign-in carries the reason in `X-Sign-In-End-Reason`; the console then shows a signed-out state with that reason, clears what the browser holds, and returns to the same address after sign-in. `POST /api/auth/logout` sends `Clear-Site-Data: "cache", "storage"` ([#4170](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4170)).

* **auth:** the common-password list is now 31,102 breached passwords of 12 or more characters (`data/common_passwords.txt`, from SecLists' `Pwdb_top-1000000` at a pinned commit; licences, filter and counts in `data/NOTICE`), up from 125. A password is refused, whole and case-insensitively, when it is on the list, is "Vigil", or is the account's username or email, and the refusal says which. This applies at bootstrap, `/change-password`, reset confirm and user creation. Sign-in re-checks the plaintext after the password and any MFA code: a password that is now listed gets no tokens, cookies or sign-in row, and `POST /api/auth/login` answers 403 with `detail.code` `password_change_required` and a reset token. The console sends the person to the reset screen with it, so `/password-reset/confirm` is the one forced-change path. Service accounts are skipped. Length, character rules, history, age and the offline import are later changes ([#4175](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4175)).

* **auth:** an agent run records the sign-in it started from (`workflow_runs.sign_in_id`, revision `0045_run_sign_in`; a handoff run inherits its source's, schedule and alert runs have none, existing runs none) and its run token carries it as `sid`. When a sign-in ends for a reason that signals compromise or a change of who the account is (`token_reuse`, `account_changed`, `deactivated`, `erased`, `ended_by_person`, `ended_by_admin`, `everyone_signed_out`; the table is `STOPS_RUNS` in `core/auth/sign_ins.py`), its in-flight runs and their children are stopped through the existing stop path and read `stopped: sign-in ended`, the run's tool calls are refused, no follow-on run (child or handoff) starts, and one notice lists the stopped runs to the person and to every holder of `users.write` (managers only after an erasure), with the count of the person's MCP credentials. Nothing is revoked. Idle, time, length-limit, eviction, sign-out and upgrade endings stop nothing, and a sign-in you act from keeps its runs unless it is itself ended. Disabling an account with `PUT /api/users/{id}` now ends its open sign-ins (`deactivated`). Runs the console starts from a chat turn have no run row, so they are cut off at their next tool call rather than stopped ([#4173](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4173)).

* **auth:** the session limits are now one stored, versioned policy (`system_config` key `auth.session_policy`) that an operator edits without a restart or redeploy: idle 1-60 minutes (15), idle for privileged accounts 1-30 and never above the standard one (10), absolute 1-24 hours (12), and an optional limit of 1-10 open sign-ins per account (off). `GET`/`PUT /api/session-policy` need the new `sessions.manage` permission, which revision `0043_session_policy` grants to built-in Admin only; `GET` returns the version as `ETag` and `PUT` requires it in `If-Match` (428 without, 412 with the current values when stale). A change applies at the next request, and every change, or refusal, is an audit entry. A service account may only tighten (403 otherwise). A new sign-in past the limit ends the account's oldest open one with reason `sign_in_limit`; service accounts are exempt. Nothing is seeded: the defaults stand until a policy is saved. ([#4186](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4186)).

* **auth:** a person can see and end their own sign-ins, and a manager can end someone else's. The account menu has *Your sign-ins* (start, last action, browser and OS, device, address, how it signed in, ended rows with why) with End, *Sign out everywhere else* and *Sign out everywhere* (`/api/auth/sign-ins`); each asks for the password, or a current authenticator or recovery code with two-step sign-in, on every call. A holder of `users.write` whose permissions cover the person's role reads and ends their sign-ins from the Users tab with a typed reason (`/api/users/{id}/sign-ins`), the person gets a notice, and a service account holding `users.write` may call the same endpoint as containment. *Sign everyone out* (`POST /api/users/sign-ins/end-all`) needs `users.write`, a person's own sign-in, a typed reason and cover for every active account's role; it ends the caller's too. Revision `0044_sign_in_end_reasons` adds three end reasons (`ended_by_person`, `ended_by_admin`, `everyone_signed_out`). The audit trail can be filtered by `sign_in` (`/api/v1/audit/entries?sign_in=…`); sign-in, sign-out and every ending entry carry the id.

* **auth:** the `sign_ins` row is now the record of whether a token is good. A request is refused when its sign-in has ended, is past 12 hours, or has had no own action for 15 minutes (10 for an account holding a privileged permission); a service account is bound by the 12 hours only. The console marks a person's own requests with `X-Vigil-User-Action`; a script's bearer call always counts, polling and streams never do. A refresh token carries a generation: the current one advances, the one before it works for 10 s (a second tab), anything older ends the sign-in (`token_reuse`) and no refresh outlives the 12 hours. The browser binding is checked against the row and the token's `sfp` claim is gone. Redis no longer holds any revocation state: `token_blacklist`, `session_cutoff` and `REVOCATION_FAIL_OPEN` (ignored if set) are removed, and a role, email or password change, a reset or a delete ends the person's sign-ins in its own transaction. Revision `0042_server_enforced_sign_ins` adds the row's columns, widens `sign_ins_end_reason_check`, and ends every open sign-in with reason `upgrade`: everyone signs in again once. `users.tokens_valid_after` stays and is unread. The limits are defaults in `core/auth/session_policy.py` for now.

* **auth:** sign-in tokens, run tokens, reset links, the first-run code and authenticator (MFA) secrets each use their own random key, kept in the new `auth_keys` table (revision `0041_auth_keys`) and wrapped under a key derived from `JWT_SECRET_KEY`, which stays outside the database and must stay set. The first API start after the upgrade adopts the ring, once across replicas: the keys derived from `JWT_SECRET_KEY` become version 1 (existing sessions, run tokens and reset links keep verifying until their overlap ends: 24 h or the refresh-token lifetime if longer, 31 d and 1 h), authenticator secrets are re-encrypted under a new random key, and new tokens carry a `kid`. Adoption, every rotation and every retirement is an audit entry. Rotate one purpose on the host with `python -m core.auth.key_ring rotate signin|run|reset|setup|mfa` (`status` lists keys); replicas pick it up within 30 seconds. `DEV_MODE` no longer has a fixed signing key: with no `JWT_SECRET_KEY`, dev generates one into the secret store; production still refuses to start without it. A restore of a database with a ring no longer changes `JWT_SECRET_KEY`: it writes new sign-in and run-token keys with no overlap, so everyone is signed out and parked runs' tokens stop working ([#4180](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4180)).

* **agents:** a custom agent is its operator manifest and nothing else. Revision `0039_custom_agent_persona_drop` fills any `custom_agents` row still without a manifest, then drops the persona columns (`name`, `description`, `icon`, `color`, `specialization`, `role`, `extra_principles`, `methodology`, `system_prompt_override`, `recommended_tools`, `max_tokens`, `enable_thinking`, `model`, `fallback_model`, `component_category`) and makes `manifest` required. `GET`/`POST /api/agents/custom` and `GET`/`PATCH`/`DELETE /api/agents/custom/{id}` are removed: read, save and delete through `/api/v1/agents`. `POST /api/agents/custom/generate` now takes and returns manifest YAML (`{"manifest": ...}`), checked as a PUT would be, and answers 422 with the reason when the check refuses the draft. `POST /api/agents/{id}/fork` forks a built-in only, into `custom-<id>`. A custom agent can no longer pin a model or fallback: it runs on the investigation assignment, and Settings › AI models no longer lists custom agents ([#4024](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4024)).

* **telemetry:** `VIGIL_OTEL_ENABLED` is gone and ignored. Traces and the OTLP metric push now start whenever `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` is non-empty, in the Python processes and the agent layer alike, and the endpoint now defaults to empty in settings, `env.example`, Compose and Helm. A `.env` copied from an older `env.example` has the endpoint set (`http://localhost:4317`), so that install starts exporting on upgrade even with `VIGIL_OTEL_ENABLED=false` still in the file; comment the line out to keep export off. On Compose, that `localhost` is the container itself, so the backend, daemon, llm-worker and agent containers log OTLP connection errors until you comment it out or set `http://otel-collector:4317`. The other way round, an install that set `VIGIL_OTEL_ENABLED=true` and left the endpoint at its old default stops exporting: set `OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` (`http://otel-collector:4317` on Compose; on Helm, `otelCollector.enabled` still sets it). `/metrics` on the backend and the daemon's `:9090` now carry Vigil's own instruments (`vigil_llm_*`, `vigil_runs_*`, ...) without any switch. `./start.sh --all` and `--with otel-collector` set the endpoint to the local collector when it is empty. A run's root span now carries `vigil.case_id` and `vigil.workflow_id`, and a tool span `gen_ai.tool.call.id`, the ledger's `call_id`; the span schema is in `docs/telemetry.md` ([#4021](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/4021)).

* **security:** Redis now requires a per-install password on Compose, host-native and Helm (in-chart Redis), so an unauthenticated client gets `NOAUTH` instead of the LLM queue, the agent run queue and the session store. Compose and host-native installs mint one into `~/.vigil/redis_password` (0600) on the next `./start.sh`, never into `.env`; a `REDIS_PASSWORD` already in the environment wins. `REDIS_URL` stays password-free and every service reads `REDIS_PASSWORD`. The start recreates a running open Redis (its data volume is kept), so stop the host processes first with `./shutdown_all.sh`. A bare `docker compose up` now fails with "REDIS_PASSWORD is empty" instead of starting an open Redis; start through `./start.sh`, or export `REDIS_PASSWORD`. Helm creates `<release>-redis-auth` on upgrade if it is absent and keeps it afterwards; under `helm template` or a GitOps controller set `redis.auth.password` (or `redis.auth.existingSecret`), and the KEDA scalers authenticate with the same Secret. Bitnami already had auth on. `REDIS_PASSWORD` replaces any password in `REDIS_URL`, so if `.env` points `REDIS_URL` at your own password-protected Redis, set `REDIS_PASSWORD` to that password (Compose's Redis then uses it too). The desktop standalone stack and the optional `misp` Redis are unchanged.

* **upgrade:** revision `0023_runtime_role_no_create` revokes `CREATE` on the `public` schema from `vigil_app`, so only `python -m core.upgrade`, logged in as the owner, creates objects there. Vigil's own processes need nothing new. Anything of your own that creates objects as `vigil_app` needs its own role; see `docs/upgrading.md`.

* **auth:** `POST /api/auth/mfa/recovery-codes` now takes `{"code": ...}`, a current authenticator code or an unused recovery code, and answers 400 without a valid one or when MFA is not on. A wrong code counts toward the sign-in lock, and a locked account gets 423. The console's Regenerate recovery codes step asks for the code.

* **auth:** every gate now checks the permission it declares; the fallback for a database without `0008_permission_catalog` is gone, and a permission check that cannot open the database refuses the call. Demo mode, theme, general settings, setup steps and AI operations under `/api/config` need `platform.read` / `platform.write`; intent and orchestrator config need `ai_autonomy.read` / `ai_autonomy.write`. `POST /api/orchestrator/enable` needs `ai_autonomy.write` and `POST /api/orchestrator/disable` needs `ai_autonomy.stop` (both keep `automation.read`), so Senior Analyst can now disable the orchestrator but not enable it. No route checks `settings.read` or `settings.write` any more; the keys stay on roles until a later revision removes them ([#3324](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/3324)).

* **intake:** a new detection now joins the open Case for its incident, whether or not that Case's run has finished: it matches on a source IP (`src_ip`/`src_ips`), a file hash, or a hostname plus the same `data_source` and title (trimmed, case-insensitive) on a finding the Case already holds. The intake row ends `merged` with reason `entity_on_open_case` (the old `overlaps_open_work` reason is gone), the alert's entities are indexed against the Case's newest investigation, the Case gets one `alert_added` activity naming the match, and its priority rises to the alert's severity when that is higher. A hostname, username, domain or destination IP alone no longer merges. A new Case is linked (`related_to`, both ways) to the 5 most recently updated closed Cases it matches by the same rules; the old cross-correlation, which linked any Cases sharing an indicator, is removed, so Cases linked only by a shared user, domain or destination IP are no longer linked. No schema change ([#3292](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/3292)).

* **audit:** once a database's trail is in use (Postgres at `0002_audit_trail` with `audit_trail` present), adding, editing and deleting a case comment, attaching a document, escalating a case, and approving or rejecting a response action each append one trail entry in the action's own transaction, naming the signed-in user's principal (the username, the previous comment text, the escalation reason and a rejection reason sit beside the hash). Those case actions then stop writing `case_audit_logs`, so the case Record tab no longer lists them; Settings › Audit does. A failed append rolls the action back. Below that revision nothing changes ([#2761](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2761)).

* **findings:** a technique confidence the source did not supply is stored as null, and readers must handle null. `true` is stored as unknown, not 1.0. `python -m core.upgrade` applies `0014_mitre_confidence_nullable`, which drops `NOT NULL` on `finding_mitre_predictions.confidence`. `PATCH /api/v1/findings/{id}` accepts a null confidence; the contract snapshot is regenerated ([#3221](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/3221)).
* **upgrade:** revision `0010_command_bar_search_indexes` adds trigram (GIN) indexes on `cases.title` and `description`, `case_comments.content`, `case_evidence.name` and `description`, `case_iocs.value`, `findings.finding_id` and `findings ((entity_context::text))`, so the command bar's case search and the findings search stop scanning those tables. It builds them without `CONCURRENTLY` inside the upgrade's own transaction, so `python -m core.upgrade` holds a write lock on each table while its index builds; size that to your largest tables. The command bar now asks full-text for 5 rows per table, so a broad query lists fewer cases, and runs only the two exact-id lookups below 3 characters. `GET /api/cases/search/full-text` and `/by-ioc` no longer hold the API's event loop ([#3202](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/3202)).
* **cases:** `POST /api/v1/cases/metrics/calculate/{case_id}` and its `/api/cases/metrics` mount are removed, and so is `scripts/calculate_case_metrics.py`. The route was beta, had no in-repo caller, and wrote a `case_metrics` table nothing reads. The `GET` metrics routes are unchanged, and the `case_metrics` table stays.
* **cases:** a case status is one of `new`, `open`, `investigating` or `closed`. `POST` and `PATCH /api/v1/cases` answer 422 for any other `status` (for example `resolved`, `Closed` or `""`), the `create_case` and `update_case` MCP tools refuse it, and the agent tools return an error. Case metrics (`/api/v1/cases/metrics/*`) and `GET /api/sla-policies/{id}/usage` count only `closed` as closed, as the queue already did, so a row stored as `resolved` before this release now counts as open. The contract snapshot and the frozen MCP tool snapshot are regenerated ([#2831](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2831)).
* **audit:** once a database is at `0002_audit_trail`, sign-ins, failed sign-ins, sign-outs, password changes and resets, MFA enable and disable, recovery-code regeneration, user create, update, role change, enable, disable and delete, MCP credential mint, revoke and first expired presentation, and the 403s on the users routes, the auth router's service-account refusal and credential mint and revoke each write a trail entry. Failed sign-ins collapse per source address and attempted name into one entry and a closing count per 60 seconds. A trail write that fails rolls back the action it records and answers 500, except sign-out, disable and an API credential revoke, which go ahead and are recorded late. A refused sign-in or 403 still answers as before when its entry can't be written. Below that revision nothing changes ([#2760](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2760)).
* **audit:** `python -m core.upgrade` applies revision `0002_audit_trail`, the first after `0001_baseline`. It adds the append-only `audit_trail` table (hash-chained by its insert trigger; `vigil_app` may read and insert, and update only the personal-detail columns beside the hash), its `audit_trail_anchor` and `audit_trail_held` tables, and `audit_trail_delete_prefix`, the only delete. It grants `audit.read` to the seeded Admin and Manager roles and `audit.forward` and `audit.manage` to Admin, keeping any value an operator already set, and lets `vigil_app` read `alembic_version`. Nothing writes to the trail yet. A fresh install from `infra/database/init` stays at the baseline schema until the upgrade runs ([#2759](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2759)).
* **upgrade:** `python -m core.upgrade` now adopts a 0.7.0 or 0.8.0 database instead of refusing it. In one transaction it repairs the known drift (tables owned by `vigil_app`, the init files Helm skipped, leftover `deeptempo` grants, legacy `admin`/`analyst`/`viewer` roles no user holds) and applies the schema changes since that release. It then stamps `0001_baseline`. It never writes `system_config.schema_version`. Any other difference in a Vigil-owned object refuses with a schema-only report and no override. A database with no stamp or another stamp is told to install a 0.7.x release first, and one that an older release re-stamped after the baseline is told to restore first. `--check` runs the adoption and rolls it back. `scripts/migrate_schema.py` is removed. Run `python -m core.upgrade` as the database owner instead. Its one-off correction of pre-#1339 stub isolations does not carry over, so on a database it never reached, mark those `isolate_host` rows `failed` by hand. See `docs/upgrading.md` ([#2740](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2740)).
* **security:** `GET /metrics` on the API port now requires `Authorization: Bearer` matching `METRICS_TOKEN`, or the file named by `METRICS_TOKEN_FILE` when the variable is unset. With neither set, every caller gets 401, including `DEV_MODE`. Compose writes a token into a volume shared with Prometheus (`--profile observability`) and scrapes with it; a `METRICS_TOKEN` you set wins and is copied into that file. The daemon's `:9090` listener is unchanged. Helm does not set a token, so an ingress in front of the API no longer serves the registry ([#2582](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2582)).
* **cases:** a comment, an evidence item, a chain-of-custody entry and a case relationship now record the signed-in user. `author`, `collected_by`, `user` and `created_by` on those requests are accepted and ignored (and are now optional). `POST /api/v1/cases/{id}/evidence` is the frozen route among them: `EvidenceAdd.collected_by` is optional, and the contract snapshot is regenerated. The console no longer writes every row as `SOC Analyst`. Agent and daemon writers are unchanged; they call the services with their own actor ([#2580](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2580)).
* **security:** CSRF is now enforced wherever `VIGIL_CSRF_REPORT_ONLY` is unset, which includes Compose, the desktop app and Helm (the chart no longer sets it to `"true"`): a `POST`, `PUT`, `PATCH` or `DELETE` whose `X-CSRF-Token` header does not match its `csrf_token` cookie answers 403 instead of only being logged. The console and the Medic gateway already send the pair, and webhooks, ingestion, `/mcp` and `/internal/` stay exempt; a script must send any value as both the cookie and the header (see `docs/operator-endpoints.md`). Set `VIGIL_CSRF_REPORT_ONLY=true` to log and allow again; on Helm, put that key back under `config` ([#2576](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2576)).
* **helm:** with the default `stateDirectory` (`emptyDir`), Settings now refuses to save a credential (integrations, S3, Claude, Darktrace, PostgreSQL, platform-database proxy, LLM provider keys) and answers 500 naming `secrets.existingSecret` and ExternalSecrets, instead of reporting success for a secret that reached one pod and was lost on restart. The chart sets `VIGIL_DIR_EPHEMERAL=true` on backend, daemon and llm-worker for an `emptyDir` only; a `persistentVolumeClaim` keeps today's behaviour. `config.SECRETS_BACKEND: "dotenv"` is removed, so writes on a shared volume use the `encrypted` store like every other install. Credentials from `secrets.existingSecret` still resolve unchanged ([#2577](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2577)).
* **helm:** with `networkPolicies.enabled`, the backend, daemon, agent-worker and agent-serve policies no longer admit `namespaceSelector: {}` (every pod in the cluster). An empty `networkPolicies.daemon.webhookAllowFrom` now admits pods in the release namespace only, and the daemon's metrics port answers only those, so a Prometheus in another namespace cannot scrape it while policies are on. `networkPolicies.agentServe.allowProbesFromAnyNamespace` is removed. Calico and Cilium let node-sourced probes through; on a CNI that does not, set `networkPolicies.probeAllowFrom` to your node CIDRs, which opens the httpGet probe ports (backend, daemon health, agent-worker 6990, agent-serve) to them and nothing else. Installs with policies off (the default) are unchanged ([#2568](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2568)).
* **llm:** Bifrost no longer stores prompt and response bodies in its request log; it keeps only the metadata Vigil reads (tokens, cost, latency, status), so cost views, budgets and recalculate-cost are unchanged. The setting applies on the next `docker compose up` or desktop launch with no action needed. Bodies already logged stay in `logs.db` in the `bifrost_data` volume (and in any `.vigil-pre-restore-*` folder a restore left there) until you remove them: stop `bifrost` and delete `/app/data/logs.db*` in the volume (this also drops cost history), or delete only the `.vigil-pre-restore-*` folders. The unused `search_logs` helper in `core.llm.bifrost.costs` is removed ([#2572](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2572)).
* **auth:** custom-agent create, edit, fork and delete, agent and workflow on/off, custom-workflow writes, Kafka settings, the federation on/off switch, per-component model assignment, and detection-rule source writes now need `settings.write`. Patching or polling one federation source needs `integrations.write`. Queueing, waking or scanning investigations needs `ai_chat.use`; killing one needs `ai_decisions.approve`. Ingesting findings needs `findings.write`, and a payload that can create cases (declared `data_type=case`, or JSON, which may carry a cases array) also needs `cases.write`. The seeded Viewer is refused. Analyst and Senior Analyst keep investigations and finding ingest; Senior Analyst keeps federation source edits and killing an investigation. `POST /api/agents/custom/generate` and `POST /api/workflows/generate` are unchanged: they return a draft and save nothing.
* **findings:** `findings.status` is no longer a lifecycle. `GET /api/v1/findings` and `GET /api/v1/findings/stats/summary` take `state` (`open`: not dismissed and in no closed case; `dismissed`; `resolved`: in a closed case and not dismissed; absent is all) in place of `status`, and the summary counts `by_state`. `PATCH /api/v1/findings/{id}` no longer accepts `status` (it is ignored), and `sort_by=status` falls back to the timestamp sort. Each finding read now carries `terminal_state`, `terminal_label` and `case_id`; the noise routes and the Overview feed item no longer return `status`. The `list_findings` and `search_findings` agent tools take `state` instead of `status`, `search_findings` and `get_findings_stats` return `by_state` instead of `by_status`, and each finding summary carries `state` (its terminal state) instead of `status`. This changes the frozen `/api/v1` and MCP surfaces; both snapshots are regenerated. The console shows a finding's state in the board's words (New, Scoring, Case NNNN, Resolved, Dropped) and offers Dismiss / Undo in place of the status select. The `findings.status` column and its `idx_finding_status` index are dropped on upgrade, and finding reads (`GET /api/v1/findings`, `GET /api/v1/findings/{id}`, findings nested on a case, the `get_finding` tool) no longer include `status` ([#2594](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2594)).
* **root cause:** every `root_cause` run now goes to the v3 graph loop (`services/agent/plugins/rootcause/v3`) instead of the kernel plugin's one-investigator trace: it holds competing causes, scores evidence, keeps "ran as" apart from "operated by", and tests whole-incident stories against the SIEM. Each move is one kernel turn on the run's own model, billed to its pool, journaled as `call`, `spend` and `frame` events, resumable after a lost lease and stoppable by an operator (the run ends `aborted` with the report so far). It reads Splunk (`splunk-selfhosted`) only: a run bound to any other telemetry tool ends `failed` naming it. `root_cause_index` in the run's config pins the index; otherwise the brief picks one, and a brief that gives no time for the event ends `failed`. The run event carries `sections.engine: rootcause-v3`, which `GET /runs/<id>/projection` and `/replay` read to answer in v3's shape (`engine`, `exit`, `report`, `moves`); runs already on the ledger keep the old shape. A root-cause run still in flight on the kernel plugin when you upgrade can't continue on v3: if it resumes, it ends `failed` ("this run was opened by the kernel plugin's root-cause loop and cannot continue on the v3 engine"). Let running root-cause runs finish, or stop them, before upgrading ([#2536](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2536)).
* **console:** Settings › AI models provider cards read the provider's base URL: a provider whose host is loopback, private (10/8, 172.16/12, 192.168/16), link-local, `host.docker.internal`, a dotless name, or ends in `.local` or `.internal` reads "Local: data stays on site" and is listed under local models. The card's second line names the host (`1 key · llm.internal:8000`, `No key needed · host`), a provider with no key shows "Not set up" and no Good or Poor level, a custom provider shows its name as typed, and the Change picker lists providers by that name rather than the `bifrost-` row id. No API change ([#2497](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2497)).
* **llm:** a provider's base URL is now set in Settings › AI models (Add provider, and an Edit provider action on each row) and lives on Bifrost's provider document; saving one also sets `allow_private_network` so a LAN or loopback server is reachable, and clearing it removes both. The catalog sync no longer clears a host Bifrost holds when the matching Vigil `openai` row has a blank `base_url`; an explicitly stock host (`https://api.openai.com/v1`) still does.
* **skills:** with `VIGIL_SKILLS_PATH` unset, operator skills now live in the State Directory's `skills/` folder: `~/.vigil/skills`, or the `vigil_home` volume on Compose and the desktop app. The folder is created (mode 0700) on the first save, so saving, importing and deleting a skill work on a fresh install, and backups copy skills with the State Directory. Set `VIGIL_SKILLS_PATH=""` to turn operator skills off, which the Helm chart does: its State Directory is a per-pod `emptyDir` unless you give it a ReadWriteMany volume (see `config.VIGIL_SKILLS_PATH` in `values.yaml`). A path you set must already exist. `POST /api/skills`, `POST /api/skills/upload` and `DELETE /api/skills/{name}` now need `settings.write` (seeded Manager and Admin), since a skill's description reaches every agent's prompt; other roles get 403 and the console hides those controls. A new skill is refused with 409 once the folder holds 100.
* **auth:** `POST /api/ai/decisions/{id}/feedback` now records the signed-in user as `human_reviewer` and answers 403 to a role that lacks `ai_decisions.approve` (the seeded Viewer and Analyst; the console already showed AI Decisions only to roles that hold it). The `human_reviewer` request field is accepted and ignored, and the console no longer asks for a name. Grant a role the permission in `roles.permissions` if it should keep grading decisions.
* **integrations:** the Network / Proxy block on Splunk, Elastic Security, OpenSearch and MISP is now applied: their HTTP clients (Test, ingestion, the daemon and the tool servers) route through the saved HTTP or SOCKS5 proxy. An explicit proxy overrides `HTTPS_PROXY`; with none saved, env behavior is unchanged. The block is now only Proxy Type (None, HTTP, SOCKS5), host, port, username and password: PgBouncer and SSH tunnel types and the SSH key and "Verify Proxy TLS" fields are gone, and a saved `pgbouncer` or `ssh_tunnel` type now behaves as None. Cribl Stream no longer shows the block (it never had a client that used it), and Splunk's proxy covers the self-hosted REST client only. Settings › System's platform-database proxy is unchanged.
* **api:** `GET`, `POST`, `PUT` and `DELETE /api/webhooks/` and its `/{webhook_id}/test` and `/{webhook_id}/deliveries` sub-routes were stubs that stored nothing and sent nothing (`DELETE` and `test` answered `success: true`); they are removed and now answer 404. The inbound receivers under `/api/webhooks/darktrace/*` and `/api/webhooks/cloudflare/*` are unchanged ([#2174](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/2174)).
* **console:** Settings › AI models now shows Keys, Spending limit and Advanced as cards under the provider cards, and the Virtual Keys and Operations tabs are gone. Spending limit edits only the one virtual key Vigil sends (ceiling, reset period, requests per minute; raising or removing the ceiling asks you to confirm); creating, listing and deleting other virtual keys is Bifrost's own UI. "Retry a local model that stops responding" moves to Advanced, with its restart and retry-count rows shown while it is on. No API change.
* **ai-config:** `PUT` and `DELETE /api/ai/config/{component}` now record the signed-in user in `ai_model_configs.updated_by` (returned as `updated_by`) and write one `config_audit_log` row (`config_type` `ai_model`, old and new `{provider_id, model_id}`) when the assignment changes. Settings › AI models replaces the Model Assignment and Custom agents tabs with provider cards and a "Model for each agent" table; changing the Chat (Default) model asks you to confirm.
* **llm:** `openrouter` is a provider type (`POST /api/llm/providers`), with models addressed as OpenRouter ids such as `anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5`. A model on it is priced from the gateway's `openrouter` datasheet, then its vendor's (`claude-haiku-5.5` looked up as `claude-haiku-5-5`), so a hunt no longer parks after one iteration on `unpriced`. A model assignment's `settings.effort` (`low`, `medium`, `high`; set per component in Settings › AI models) is sent to OpenRouter as `reasoning: {effort}` and to Anthropic as `output_config.effort`, on hunts and document condensing; other components still run at the model's own default ([#1931](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1931)).
* **hunt:** the hypothesis `/hunt` proposes from an attached document is now the document's own: its stated hunting hypothesis verbatim, else one testable sentence the `summarization` model writes from it, and the indicator template only when neither is available. A document already being hunted still gets a proposal, with an "Open its case" door to the running hunt; `POST /api/workflows/threat-hunt/coverage` now returns a `proposal` for a `running` report too, and each `in_flight` row carries its `case_id` ([#1918](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1918)). The `check_hunt_coverage` agent tool is unchanged.
* **orchestrator:** `OrchestratorSettingsConfig` now has bounds, so `POST /api/config/orchestrator` answers 422 for a value outside them (for example `max_concurrent_agents` 0 or 1000). `GET /api/config/orchestrator` also serves `defaults` and `bounds`, and still loads a config saved before the bounds existed so it can be corrected. 0 never meant unlimited to the daemon; it is the tightest cap, so Settings › Limits and autonomy no longer offers "Unlimited" ([#1648](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1648)).
* **skills:** `POST /api/skills` now answers 422 when `body` (the Steps) is empty or whitespace, including when saving a copy of a skill under a new name with `source`; send the steps with the copy. Skills already on disk with an empty body still load and list. The skill drawer keeps Save off until When to use it and Steps are filled, and says which is missing ([#1879](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1879)).
* **hunt:** `/hunt` takes an attached document (PDF, DOCX, PPTX, XLSX, PNG, JPG, Markdown, text or HTML, up to 25 MB), read on the server by LiteParse ([#1860](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1860)). Office files need LibreOffice on the API host (the images do not ship it), and OCR for images and scanned pages needs Tesseract's `eng.traineddata`, which LiteParse downloads from GitHub on first use: an air-gapped install must put that file in a folder and set `TESSDATA_PREFIX` to it, or only text-layer PDFs, Markdown, text and HTML can be attached. Text over 64 KB is condensed on the `summarization` model, so assign one in Settings first.
* **ai-config:** `PUT` and `DELETE /api/ai/config/{component}` now record the signed-in user in `ai_model_configs.updated_by` (returned as `updated_by`) and write one `config_audit_log` row (`config_type` `ai_model`, old and new `{provider_id, model_id}`) when the assignment changes. Settings › AI models replaces the Model Assignment and Custom agents tabs with provider cards and a "Model for each agent" table; changing the Chat (Default) model asks you to confirm.
* **sla:** Settings › SLA policies is a board table with an inline editor and hours-and-minutes duration pickers, and `GET /api/sla-policies/{id}/usage` takes an optional `since` (the table's "Met this month" passes the start of the UTC month). The editor no longer edits `notification_thresholds`, which nothing reads; the table shows the stored values read-only, and the console no longer writes them. Set a policy as default with its "Default for this severity" switch.
* **auth:** `POST /api/orchestrator/enable|disable|kill|investigations/purge` and `PUT /api/analytics/budget` now need `settings.write`, and `DELETE /api/findings/all` needs `findings.delete`; the two wipes write the signed-in user to the config audit log first. `POST /api/config/integrations` answers 400 when a URL, host, region or similar field changes and a stored credential of that integration is not sent again, so a saved credential is never carried to a new destination; enter the credential together with the new URL.
* **orchestrator:** a completed investigation that belongs to a case no longer creates a `case-review` investigation. No `case-review` workflow exists, so each one failed at enqueue and left an `assigned` row and workdir behind ([#1841](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1841)). Rows already created stay as they are; delete any `workflow_id = 'case-review'` investigations you do not want.
* **auth:** `POST /api/auth/logout` now also revokes a refresh token sent in the request body (`{"refresh_token": ...}`), so a Bearer-flow client can end its refresh token at logout; it was revoked only when it arrived in the cookie. Send it when logging out.
* **api:** the Jira export, the bulk-enrich results and the VStrike inbound push no longer put an exception's text in their response: a failed Jira export answers `JIRA API error: HTTP <status>`, `JIRA API error: could not reach Jira` or `Internal server error`, and a failed item answers `Internal server error` (the detail is in the server log). The exception-text ratchet now also covers response fields set by keyword and error lists.
* **auth:** a refresh token now rotates: `POST /api/auth/refresh` accepts each one once (a replay within 10 seconds, from a browser with several tabs, still succeeds) and answers 401 afterwards, so a client must keep the refresh token the last call returned. The per-user "revoked before" cutoff in Redis now expires after `JWT_REFRESH_EXPIRATION_DAYS`, and the API logs an error at startup when Redis runs an evicting `maxmemory-policy`, since an evicted revocation key reads as not revoked. Run an external Redis with `noeviction`.
* **api:** a failure the API did not expect no longer returns its exception text (database host, SQL, upstream bodies, file paths) to the caller. Orchestrator, VStrike, custom-agent, custom-integration, detection-rule and LLM-provider routes answer `Internal server error` with a `trace_id` (the detail is in the server log); `/api/config/*`, `/api/storage/*`, the insights refresh and the chat stream carry that same text in their `error`/`message` field. Typed 4xx errors keep their messages.
* **integrations:** a Custom Integration's password-typed fields are now stored in the encrypted secrets store, like a built-in integration's, and are no longer returned by `GET /api/config/integrations`, written to the database row, the audit log or `integrations_config.json`. A value saved earlier stays in those places until the integration is saved again (the next save moves it to the secrets store under `<CUSTOM_ID>_<FIELD>`), and in `config_audit_log` history: rotate any credential you entered. A generated server must read a credential with `resolve_fields` (the Builder now generates that); one that reads it from `get_integration_config` gets nothing once the field is stripped.
* **orchestrator:** `POST /api/orchestrator/kill` now writes `orchestrator.settings.enabled = false`, so the daemon stops launching investigations (it did not before), and records the signed-in user in the config audit log. It still does not cancel a run already executing on the agent worker. When the daemon cannot count in-flight investigations or read the hour's spend, it now holds intake instead of launching without a limit.
* **memory:** the Distil now strips control characters from, and caps at 2000 characters, the statement, rationale and reason it stores, and a run's recalled notes reach the Hunt Lead scrubbed, capped and inside a `<vigil:recalled_memory>` block. Rows stored earlier are bounded when read, so nothing needs re-deriving. A replay of a run recorded before this change rebuilds the opening turn in the new form, so its bytes differ from what that run was shown.
* **auth:** only an access token now authenticates `/api/*` routes. A refresh token, or a signed token with a missing or unknown `token_type`, presented as a Bearer header or in the `access_token` cookie answers 401. A script that sent its refresh token as the Bearer credential must send the access token, and use `POST /api/auth/refresh` to renew it ([#1712](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1712)).
* **splunk:** the Splunk integration (and the Cribl Stream form) now verifies the server's TLS certificate unless you turn it off. An install that never set "Verify SSL Certificate" now verifies, so a Splunk on port 8089 with its default self-signed certificate stops connecting: set the CA Certificate Path (`SPLUNK_CA_CERT_PATH`) to the CA that signed it, or set `verify_ssl` to false (`SPLUNK_VERIFY_SSL=false`) to keep the old behaviour. A stored `verify_ssl: false` is left as it is.
* **response:** isolations recorded before [#1339](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/1339) were never executed: the stub reported success without isolating the host. `python scripts/migrate_schema.py` now marks those `isolate_host` rows `failed` (`execution_result.message` keeps the original `(MOCK)` text and gains an `error`), and logs the hosts. Those hosts were not contained, and a new isolation for one of them is no longer answered with "already isolated" ([#1686](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1686)).
* **auth:** role permissions are now enforced on writes, not only on login. Case, finding, approval, agent-run and `/api/config` writes, chat, and the chat/MCP `approve_action`/`reject_action` tools answer 403 to a role that lacks `cases.write`, `findings.write`, `ai_decisions.approve`, `ai_chat.use` or `settings.write`/`integrations.write` (the seeded Viewer is read-only; Analyst and Senior Analyst no longer change settings). Approvals, merges and run directives record the signed-in user; the `approved_by`, `rejected_by`, `merged_by` and `actor` request fields are accepted and ignored. The `POST /api/workflows/runs/{id}/resume` route is removed: approve a paused run's action through `POST /api/v1/approvals/{id}/approve`. Grant a role the permission in `roles.permissions` if it should keep an action.
* **approvals:** a confidence an agent or the daemon's triage model names no longer releases a response action. `create_approval_action` (MCP and chat) always queues the action for a person and refuses a confidence outside 0..1; the daemon's triage reply is ignored for a confidence outside 0..1, a repeated key or an action outside isolate/block/investigate/monitor/dismiss; and the executor leaves alone an approved action no person decided. Agent prompts no longer promise auto-approval. Operator directive text is capped at 2000 characters.
* **workflows:** the workflow listing (`GET /api/workflows`, `/api/v1/workflows`) reports the last 7 days instead of today: `runs_today` is gone, replaced by `runs_7d`, `success_rate` (a 0..1 fraction) and `success_level` (good/fair/poor), and `mean_cost_usd` now averages the finished runs of the same window ([#1624](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1624)). The Workflows tab is a column of cards in place of the table; its Agents, Last edited and Trust columns are gone.
* **workflows:** each workflow now has an on/off switch in the console (`PUT /api/workflows/{id}/enabled`, stored as `workflows.disabled`), and nothing starts a workflow while it is off ([#1623](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1623)). `DAEMON_THREAT_HUNT_ENABLED` and `ORCHESTRATOR_SHADOW_ADJUDICATION` are gone from the environment, compose and Helm values; setting them has no effect. Shadow adjudication is off until you turn `shadow-adjudication` on. `incident-response` (where alerts land when nothing else fits) and `threat-hunt` (the scheduled and threat-intel hunts) cannot be turned off, and a `workflows.disabled` row that lists either has no effect ([#3468](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/3468)).
* **logging:** the backend, daemon and llm-worker now write one JSON object per line to stdout and `vigil.log` whether or not `VIGIL_OTEL_ENABLED` is set, each with a `msg_template` field (and `exc_type` on exceptions) for grouping ([#1563](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1563)). Set `VIGIL_LOG_FORMAT=text` for the old plain format; `./start.sh` in the foreground defaults to text. The daemon's `log_format` is gone. Third-party loggers (httpx, httpcore, sqlalchemy.engine, urllib3, watchfiles, asyncio) are pinned at WARNING.
* **compose:** the State Directory (`master.key`, `secrets.enc`) and `data/investigations` now live in the `vigil_home` and `vigil_investigations` named volumes, shared by `backend`, `soc-daemon` and `llm-worker` ([#1263](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1263)). The first `up` after this change recreates those containers and drops whatever is still in their writable layers. Save the backend's copy first: `docker cp deeptempo-backend:/home/vigil/.vigil ./vigil-state-backup`. After the upgrade, copy it into the volume and restart: `docker cp ./vigil-state-backup/. deeptempo-backend:/home/vigil/.vigil/ && docker exec -u 0 deeptempo-backend chown -R 1000:1000 /home/vigil/.vigil && docker compose restart backend soc-daemon llm-worker`.
* **desktop:** Bifrost's runtime settings (provider keys, virtual keys, budgets, rate limits) now persist in a `bifrost_data` named volume, so they survive quitting the app ([#1452](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1452)). Earlier versions kept them in an anonymous volume that every quit orphaned; those are not migrated, so re-enter any Bifrost settings made in its UI once. Providers seeded from environment variables come back on their own. Reclaim the orphaned volumes with `docker volume prune`.
* **compose:** Bifrost's request log (`logs.db`) now lives in the `bifrost_data` volume, so gateway log history, cost time series and `recalculate-cost` survive a container recreate ([#1454](https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/issues/1454)). It was written to `/app/logs.db` in the container's writable layer. Applies to the server and desktop standalone stacks. The config store is untouched, so no volume reset is needed. History already in `/app/logs.db` is not migrated: the first recreate drops it, as every recreate did before. Snapshots still leave `logs.db` out, and a restore moves the live one aside into `.pre-restore-<stamp>` in the Bifrost data directory rather than restoring it.

## 1.2.0 (2026-11-27)

## What's Changed
* Clear invite addresses when an account is erased by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4858
* Index a finding's single src_ip, dst_ip or hostname as the plain value by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4856
* Fence the answer under review in a vigil:answer block by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4854
* Judge federation stall and silence against each source's poll interval by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4864
* Keep directory sync from pushing reserved usernames by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4860
* Delete notices other people received about an erased person by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4862
* Send an intake-merged alert back to intake when it is taken off the case by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4867
* Link or unlink one case alert without dropping a concurrent attach by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4865
* Bind SSO account lookups to identifiers a provider user can't steer by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4863
* Keep Custom Integration secrets out of the platform's secret names by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4879
* Delete erased people's notices when a restore re-applies the erasure by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4882
* Keep one Integration Health record per source by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4883
* Keep refused directory deactivations and role changes listed until applied by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4881
* Say when a saved custom integration is not running by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4889
* Test threat-intel, sandbox, and lookup credentials with one fixed read by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4894
* Refuse side-effecting SPL in Splunk searches by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4888
* Keep erasures in start.sh backups and out of the restore notice by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4897
* Keep every closed ticket of a history import in its own record by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4898
* Count a dismissal's independent sources in code before a case can close as benign by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4899
* docs(readme): GA status, badges, and What Vigil is not section by @johnvanlowe in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4906
* Rewrite an erased person's username in every record that stores it by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4902
* Show a history import's full result, and offer it on "Connect your data" by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4905
* Merge hunt sightings that mint the same Entity Key before the Distil writes them by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4907
* Keep usernames out of the principal-id namespace, and name trail actors by principal id by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4908
* Show Install Readiness live on Home by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4904
* Push sources write Integration Health by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4896
* Fixed read-only Test calls for identity, EDR, network and notification integrations by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4895
* Console health displays read the server's Integration Health by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4900
* Let Stop and floor demotions land during a restore's write pause by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4909
* Record alert-source, MCP server and custom-integration changes on the audit trail by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4910
* Stamp the cases list's last_activity as UTC by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4920
* Ask "Confirm it's you" in the setup wizard instead of a raw 403 by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4921
* Deployment-wide operator instructions for every agent run by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4922
* Let agents page through a finding's stored evidence records by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4923
* Move the instance signing key out of the proof card into core.auth.signing_key by @G-r-ay in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4931
* Add the cases.export permission by @G-r-ay in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4932
* Stage host inputs before restic reads them (macOS VirtioFS EIO) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4933
* File no proposals when a guard holds the answer by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4942
* Fold verdict bursts before recall's per-key cap by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4943
* skills: install and update from private Git repos on allowed HTTPS hosts by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4944
* Count returned, stored and filtered records per federation window (#4940) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4945
* cases: one headline for an agent close, the report once as Markdown (#4946) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4947
* Land Dependabot bumps (#4948–#4959) with regenerated requirements.lock by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4960
* agents: a suspicious conclusion passes the escalation guard, and no guard falls back to an alarm (#4961) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4968
* cases: a capped, failed or uncited run no longer becomes the case's run over one that stands (#4962) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4969
* cases: record false_positive_cause=expected when an agent closes on benign (#4964) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4970
* approvals: a proposal's target must fit its action type, and a zero confidence is not a claim (#4963) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4971
* agents: name folded tool results by ref or call id, tool and subject (#4966) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4972
* proposal_targets: linear-time path/URL patterns and a length cap in target_kind by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4973
* fix(#4974): OpenCTI get_related also lists the relationships that point at the entity by @G-r-ay in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4975
* fix(#4976): the integration setup panel offers Collect alerts and Automatic investigations only for an alert source by @G-r-ay in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4979
* fix(#4977): an integration whose server is switched off reads Off, not Good by @G-r-ay in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4980
* test(#4978): a weekly live check runs OpenCTI's tools against a local platform by @G-r-ay in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4981
* fix(#4977): a switched-off alert source's row says its collection is still on by @G-r-ay in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4982
* Store the Day 1 Report and show what Vigil can see by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4917
* Store Splunk and CrowdStrike informational findings as info by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4913
* Clear a source severity floor when Alert collection sends null by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4911
* Say what Alert collection's master switch actually stops by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4912
* Pin the Alembic head so two revision PRs cannot both merge by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4988
* Check Vigil's own settings against their cited values by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4989
* Report detection coverage gaps on the Day 1 Report by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4990
* Start CrowdStrike's tool server from credentials saved in Settings by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4993
* Medic sees each source's floor, cap, interval, and floor skips by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4994
* Day 1 Report: module switches, comparison, and a one-page summary by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4991
* Read CrowdStrike alerts through the Alerts API by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/5005
* Keep a collection failure run through a successful lookup or re-check by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/5003
* Suggest a dedicated Wazuh API user instead of wazuh-wui by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/5004
* Bound memory and event-loop use in file and string ingestion by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/5006
* feat(#4983): the person who closes a case owns it by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4984
* Enforce platform DB proxy TLS and require the DB login to move the proxy by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/5014
* Keep alert-supplied user names inside one Graph path segment and one OData literal in the Entra ID tools by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/5020

* A held supervisory suggestion stays held when its check suggests it again while Medic can't be read by @G-r-ay in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/5352
* The supervisory page says when a reload failed, with a Retry by @G-r-ay in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/5350


**Full Changelog**: https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/compare/v1.1.4...v1.2.0

## 1.1.4 (2026-11-19)

## What's Changed
* Serve /mcp stateless, so a client's requests work across API workers and replicas by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4811
* Ask Vigil follows the run on its case: a live run block drawn by Watch run's step cards, started from the dock with /hunt or /investigate (#4790) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4812
* A hunt can search the Compose Elastic profile, project metadata fields and aggregate (#4795) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4813
* A finding on the insider lead list opens a restricted threat-hunt case on its account by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4816
* OAuth: approve a request, issue and rotate tokens, and /mcp accepts them (#4788) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4817
* Retire the legacy per-Investigation subject of the shared IOC index (#4815) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4821
* OAuth consent page, connected apps in the tokens list, and the redirect allowlist field (#4789) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4823
* A weekly run measures four scale limits of one install by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4822
* Document OAuth on the MCP surface, and how the plugin signs in (#4777) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4827
* Console close forms ask what was decided and preselect nothing (#4820) by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4829
* Every close a model chooses passes the autonomy setting; the close trail says who closed and as what by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4830
* Publish a daily beta of main's images, and add a stable tag to releases by @nestor-deeptempo in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4828
* Release minors by hand from main; fix releases from a release/x.y.x branch by @nestor-deeptempo in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4832
* Copy fixes labeled backport onto the release branch, and keep database changes out of it by @nestor-deeptempo in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4833
* Delete beta images older than 14 days from ghcr, without touching stable ones by @nestor-deeptempo in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4834
* /revoke works for public clients; an expiring grant's notice names the connected app by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4835
* Helm: keep Bifrost's admin password out of the agent pods by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4836
* Require SSO: an open password sign-in can't open Confirm it's you to mint a token by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4837
* Hold an unattended run's act when its workflow can't be read by @samarmstrong in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4838
* State why user and email Entity Keys are case-folded by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4846
* End open non-SSO sign-ins when Require single sign-on is turned on by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4844
* Look file hashes up through lookup_indicators (#4840) by @craig-dt in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4843
* Skills from one repo show as one stacked library card on the Skills tab by @G-r-ay in https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/pull/4859


**Full Changelog**: https://github.com/Vigil-SOC/vigil/compare/v1.1.3...v1.1.4
